3. Write out Romans 8:33–34 and read it aloud when accusation comes.
4. Share one area of accusation with a trusted believer and ask them to remind you of the gospel.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Satan accuses believers day and night, using real failures to push them into despair and paralysis. The blood of Jesus and the advocacy of Christ close the case against every repentant believer.

**Key principle:** "Who shall lay any thing to the charge of God's elect? It is God that justifieth. Who is he that condemneth? It is Christ that died, yea rather, that is risen again, who also maketh intercession for us" (Rom. 8:33-34).

**Core teaching points:**
- Satan's name, *diabolos*, means slanderer or false accuser (Rev. 12:10).
- His accusations often contain facts but always draw false conclusions about identity and future.
- Zechariah's vision shows filthy garments removed and clean garments given because of redemption (Zech. 3:1-4).
- Holy Spirit conviction is specific and redemptive; Satanic accusation is vague, heavy, and hopeless.
- The blood of Jesus cleanses from all sin and answers every charge (1 John 1:7, 9; Rom. 8:33-34).
- Believers silence the accuser by confessing real sin, preaching the gospel to themselves, and refusing to rehearse the past.

**Real-world example:** A person who once failed publicly in ministry believes the thought "You can never preach again." The thought feels true because the failure was real, but it leads to hiding from God instead of returning to the cross.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Rehearsing past failures, accepting vague shame as truth, and letting accusation replace honest confession.

**Practice assignment:** Write out Romans 8:33-34 on a card and read it aloud the next time accusation comes. Share one area of accusation with a trusted believer and ask them to remind you of the gospel.

**Worksheet idea:** Draw two columns labeled "Conviction (Holy Spirit)" and "Accusation (Satan)." Place a recent guilty thought in the correct column and write the Scripture that answers each voice.

**Completion requirement:** The student confesses any real sin, receives 1 John 1:9 by faith, and shares one accusation with a trusted believer for a gospel reminder.

---

## Questions on Lesson 16 — Satan as Accuser: How He Uses Guilt and Shame

- How does accusation differ from conviction?
ANSWER: Conviction is specific, clean, and leads to repentance; accusation is vague, heavy, and leads to despair.
- What is the believer's answer to every accusation?
ANSWER: The blood of Jesus, God's justification, and Christ's intercession.
- What practice breaks the isolation that accusation creates?
ANSWER: Confessing faults to a trusted believer (James 5:16).



---

### Lesson 17 — Temptation: How the Devil Tests the Saints

## 1. Temptation Is a Test, Not a Sin

Every believer faces temptation. Many assume that being tempted means they are failing spiritually. That is not true. James wrote, "Blessed is the man that endureth temptation: for when he is tried, he shall receive the crown of life, which the Lord hath promised to them that love him" (James 1:12). Temptation is a test. Passing the test produces endurance and reward.

James also makes a critical distinction: "Let no man say when he is tempted, I am tempted of God: for God cannot be tempted with evil, neither tempteth he any man: but every man is tempted, when he is drawn away of his own lust, and enticed" (James 1:13–14). God tests faith, as He tested Abraham. Satan tempts to sin. The difference is in the goal. God tests to prove and strengthen. Satan tempts to destroy.

The important thing is what you do with the temptation. A thought that enters your mind is not a sin. A desire that knocks at the door is not a sin. Sin begins when you open the door and invite the desire inside. James describes the progression clearly: "Then when lust hath conceived, it bringeth forth sin: and sin, when it is finished, bringeth forth death" (James 1:15). Conception is the moment of agreement. Death is the final payment.

## 2. The Temptation of Jesus: The Pattern

The temptation of Jesus in the wilderness is the model for every believer. Matthew 4 and Luke 4 record the confrontation. After forty days of fasting, Jesus was hungry, tired, and physically weakened. Satan came at the point of weakness. He always does.

The first temptation was physical. "Command that these stones be made bread" (Matt. 4:3). The second was spectacle. Satan took Jesus to the pinnacle of the temple and said, "Cast thyself down" (Matt. 4:6). The third was power. Satan showed Jesus all the kingdoms of the world and said, "All these things will I give thee, if thou wilt fall down and worship me" (Matt. 4:9).

Jesus answered every temptation with Scripture. "It is written, Man shall not live by bread alone, but by every word that proceedeth out of the mouth of God" (Matt. 4:4). "It is written again, Thou shalt not tempt the Lord thy God" (Matt. 4:7). "Get thee hence, Satan: for it is written, Thou shalt worship the Lord thy God, and him only shalt thou serve" (Matt. 4:10). Three times the devil offered a shortcut. Three times Jesus answered with the written Word. Then the devil left Him.

This is the pattern for our victory. We do not overcome Satan by willpower, by emotion, or by clever argument. We overcome by the truth of God spoken in faith.

## 3. The Three Lusts

The apostle John summarized the world system under three categories: "the lust of the flesh, and the lust of the eyes, and the pride of life" (1 John 2:16). These three lusts were present in Eden, in the wilderness, and in your life today.

**The lust of the flesh** is the desire to feel pleasure, comfort, or relief apart from God's order. It says, "I want this sensation now." It includes sexual immorality, gluttony, drunkenness, drug use, and any demand for immediate physical gratification. Satan tempted Jesus to turn stones into bread because Jesus was hungry. The desire was legitimate; the shortcut was not.

**The lust of the eyes** is the desire to possess what you see. It says, "I want that thing." It is the love of money, the envy of another's house, the craving for status symbols, and the constant comparison that social media feeds. When Satan showed Jesus the kingdoms of the world, he was appealing to the lust of the eyes.

**The pride of life** is the desire to be admired, exalted, or recognized. It says, "I want to be great." Satan tempted Jesus to cast Himself down from the temple so the angels would rescue Him publicly. The crowd would be amazed. Jesus would have gained fame without the cross.

Every temptation you face will fit into one or more of these categories. Learn to name them. When you can name the lust, you can refuse it.

## 4. The Progression: Thought, Action, Consequence

Temptation moves from thought to action to consequence. Understanding this progression helps you stop it early.

It begins with a thought. A memory, an image, a suggestion, or a circumstance triggers a desire. At this stage, the temptation is still outside the door. You can reject it immediately. Paul told the Corinthians to "cast down imaginations, and every high thing that exalteth itself against the knowledge of God, and bringing into captivity every thought to the obedience of Christ" (2 Cor. 10:5). Take the thought captive before it takes you captive.

If the thought is entertained, it becomes a desire. You begin to imagine what it would be like. You justify it. You compare it to other sins that seem worse. You tell yourself it would not really matter. This is the stage of negotiation. Eve saw that the tree was good for food, pleasant to the eyes, and desirable to make one wise (Gen. 3:6). She entertained the desire.

Then comes action. You eat the fruit. You send the message. You make the purchase. You cross the line. The moment of action feels like freedom, but it is actually bondage. The desire that promised life brings death.

Finally comes consequence. "Sin, when it is finished, bringeth forth death" (James 1:15). The consequence may be immediate or delayed, visible or hidden, physical or spiritual. But it always comes. The wages of sin is death (Rom. 6:23). The believer who understands this progression will refuse the thought rather than manage the action.

## 5. The Promise of Escape

God has made a remarkable promise to every tempted believer. "There hath no temptation taken you but such as is common to man: but God is faithful, who will not suffer you to be tempted above that ye are able; but will with the temptation also make a way to escape, that ye may be able to bear it" (1 Cor. 10:13).

This verse contains several encouragements. First, your temptation is not unique. Others have faced the same thing and endured. You are not the first person to struggle with this. Second, God is faithful. He will not allow you to be tested beyond your strength. He knows your frame; He remembers that you are dust (Ps. 103:14). Third, He provides a way of escape. The escape is not always dramatic. Sometimes it is simply the ability to say no and walk away.

The way of escape is real, but you must take it. God will not force you out of temptation. He opens the door; you must walk through it.

## 6. How to Flee

The Bible does not tell believers to debate with temptation. It tells them to flee. Paul told Timothy to "flee also youthful lusts" (2 Tim. 2:22). He told the Corinthians to "flee fornication" (1 Cor. 6:18). Joseph did not negotiate with Potiphar's wife. He ran, leaving his garment behind (Gen. 39:12). Running is not cowardice. It is wisdom.

To flee effectively, you must remove the fuel. Do not keep the temptation before your eyes. Do not revisit the website, the memory, the relationship, or the conversation that ignites the desire. Make practical boundaries. Put filters on devices. End compromising friendships. Avoid the places and situations that weaken you.

Fill the empty space with better things. Paul told the Philippians, "Whatsoever things are true, whatsoever things are honest, whatsoever things are just, whatsoever things are pure, whatsoever things are lovely, whatsoever things are of good report; if there be any virtue, and if there be any praise, think on these things" (Phil. 4:8). You cannot empty your mind by willpower. You must fill it with truth.

Finally, pray before the temptation comes. Jesus taught His disciples to pray, "Lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil" (Matt. 6:13). This is not a prayer of weakness. It is a prayer of humility. It recognizes that you need divine help to stay out of danger.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Identify which of the three lusts most often trips you up: lust of the flesh, lust of the eyes, or pride of life.
2. Name one practical way of escape you will use the next time that temptation appears.
3. Memorize 1 Corinthians 10:13 and quote it when temptation begins.
4. Pray Matthew 6:13 every morning this week: "Lead me not into temptation, but deliver me from evil."

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Temptation is a test, not a sin. Believers overcome by naming the lust involved, fleeing rather than negotiating, and taking the way of escape that God provides.

**Key principle:** "There hath no temptation taken you but such as is common to man: but God is faithful, who will not suffer you to be tempted above that ye are able; but will with the temptation also make a way to escape, that ye may be able to bear it" (1 Cor. 10:13).

**Core teaching points:**
- Being tempted is not the same as sinning; sin begins when lust conceives and agrees (James 1:13-15).
- God tests to strengthen; Satan tempts to destroy.
- Jesus answered each wilderness temptation with Scripture, providing the pattern for victory (Matt. 4:1-11).
- Every temptation fits one or more of the three lusts: flesh, eyes, and pride of life (1 John 2:16).
- Temptation progresses from thought to desire to action to consequence.
- God promises a way of escape with every temptation (1 Cor. 10:13).
- The biblical response is to flee, remove fuel, and fill the mind with truth (2 Tim. 2:22; Phil. 4:8).

**Real-world example:** A tired employee receives a late-night message from a former relationship. Instead of reasoning about it, the person blocks the contact, calls an accountability partner, and quotes 1 Corinthians 10:13.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Trying to debate with temptation, manage it, or prove strength instead of fleeing and removing the fuel.

**Practice assignment:** Identify which of the three lusts most often trips you up and create one concrete escape plan you will use the next time that temptation appears.

**Worksheet idea:** Map your last three temptations: trigger, lust category, thought, action taken or avoided, consequence, Scripture used, and the escape route you will use next time.

**Completion requirement:** The student names the dominant lust, implements one escape plan, and quotes 1 Corinthians 10:13 when temptation comes.

---

## Questions on Lesson 17 — Temptation: How the Devil Tests the Saints

- What is the difference between being tempted and sinning?
ANSWER: Temptation is the test; sin begins when lust is entertained and conceives.
- What are the three lusts that summarize the world system?
ANSWER: The lust of the flesh, the lust of the eyes, and the pride of life.
- What should a believer do when temptation appears?
ANSWER: Flee, take the way of escape, and answer with Scripture.



---

### Lesson 18 — The Bait of Satan: Offense, Unforgiveness, and Bitterness

## 1. The Trap Called Skandalon

The New Testament uses a vivid word for offense. It is the Greek word *skandalon*, which referred to the stick or trigger in a trap that held the bait. When an animal touched the bait, the stick moved, and the trap snapped shut. Offense works the same way in the spiritual world. Something happens — a word, an action, a neglect — and we take the bait. We grab hold of the offense, and suddenly we are caught.

Paul used this language when writing to the Corinthians. He urged them to forgive and comfort the one who had sinned, "lest perhaps such a one should be swallowed up with overmuch sorrow" (2 Cor. 2:7). Then he added the reason: "To whom ye forgive any thing, I forgive also: for if I forgave any thing, to whom I forgave it, for your sakes forgave I it in the person of Christ; lest Satan should get an advantage of us: for we are not ignorant of his devices" (2 Cor. 2:10–11). Unforgiveness gives Satan an advantage. It is one of his devices.

The bait of Satan is offense. He knows that every human being will be hurt, slighted, misunderstood, or wronged. His strategy is to keep you holding onto the hurt until it becomes something far worse.

## 2. How Offense Becomes Bait

Offense begins with a real or perceived wrong. Someone spoke harshly. Someone failed to keep a promise. Someone betrayed a confidence. Someone received recognition you deserved. The event is the bait. Your reaction determines whether the trap closes.

The first response is usually anger or hurt. That response is not sin by itself. Jesus was grieved at the hardness of men's hearts (Mark 3:5). The problem comes when you hold the offense. You rehearse it. You tell others about it. You build a case. You wait for the offender to come crawling back. In doing so, you swallow the bait.

Satan whispers three lies to keep the offense alive. First, "You have a right to be angry." Second, "If you forgive, you are letting them off the hook." Third, "They do not deserve your forgiveness." All three are bait. Forgiveness is not about the offender's worthiness. It is about your freedom.

## 3. From Offense to Bitterness

If offense is not released, it becomes bitterness. The writer of Hebrews warns, "Looking diligently lest any man fail of the grace of God; lest any root of bitterness springing up trouble you, and thereby many be defiled" (Heb. 12:15). Bitterness is not a surface weed. It is a root. It grows underground, out of sight, and sends up shoots in unexpected places.

Bitterness defiles many. A bitter person pollutes his family, his church, his workplace, and his friendships. The bitterness spreads through complaints, sarcasm, coldness, and gossip. People who were not part of the original offense begin to carry the poison.

Bitterness also hardens the heart. Ephesians 4:31–32 commands, "Let all bitterness, and wrath, and anger, and clamour, and evil speaking, be put away from you, with all malice: and be ye kind one to another, tenderhearted, forgiving one another, even as God for Christ's sake hath forgiven you." Bitterness is not a private emotion. It is sin that must be put away.

## 4. The Cost of Unforgiveness

Unforgiveness is expensive. It costs you peace, sleep, health, relationships, and spiritual authority. Jesus told a parable about a servant who owed his king ten thousand talents and was forgiven, but who refused to forgive a fellow servant who owed him a hundred pence. When the king heard this, he delivered the unforgiving servant to the tormentors (Matt. 18:23–35). Jesus concluded, "So likewise shall my heavenly Father do also unto you, if ye from your hearts forgive not every one his brother their trespasses" (Matt. 18:35).

The tormentors in that parable are real. Unforgiving people are tormented by their own thoughts. They replay conversations. They plan speeches they will never deliver. They punish themselves by refusing to let go. Forgiveness is the door out of the prison.

Unforgiveness also gives Satan a foothold. Paul told the Ephesians, "Neither give place to the devil" (Eph. 4:27). The word "place" means a foothold or a beachhead. Holding offense is like handing the enemy a base of operations in your soul. From that base he can launch accusations, temptations, and deceptions.

## 5. The Path to Forgiveness

Forgiveness is a decision before it is a feeling. You do not wait until you feel like forgiving. You choose to forgive because Christ forgave you. Ephesians 4:32 makes this plain: "Forgiving one another, even as God for Christ's sake hath forgiven you." The standard is not the size of the offense. The standard is the grace you have received.

The first step is to release the debt. The person who hurt you owes you something — an apology, justice, restoration, acknowledgment. Forgiveness means you cancel the debt. You stop demanding payment. You hand the matter over to God, who said, "Vengeance is mine; I will repay" (Rom. 12:19).

The second step is to stop rehearsing the offense. Do not keep bringing it up in your own mind or in conversation with others. When the memory comes, make a deliberate choice to think about something else. Philippians 4:8 provides the replacement thoughts.

The third step is to pray for the offender. Jesus commanded, "Love your enemies, bless them that curse you, do good to them that hate you, and pray for them which despitefully use you, and persecute you" (Matt. 5:44). Prayer for an enemy is one of the most powerful acts of freedom. It is hard at first, but it breaks the grip of bitterness.

The fourth step is to seek reconciliation where possible. Romans 12:18 says, "If it be possible, as much as lieth in you, live peaceably with all men." Reconciliation requires both parties, and sometimes it is not safe or possible. But if it is possible, pursue it.

## 6. Reconciliation and Boundaries

Forgiveness and reconciliation are related but not identical. Forgiveness is a command and is always required. Reconciliation is a process and requires repentance and safety. You can forgive someone while maintaining necessary boundaries.

If the offender is unrepentant, dangerous, or manipulative, you are not required to return to the same relationship. Proverbs 22:3 says, "A prudent man foreseeth the evil, and hideth himself: but the simple pass on, and are punished." Wisdom protects the innocent. Forgiveness does not mean trust is automatically restored. Trust is rebuilt through repentance and changed behavior over time.

But even when reconciliation is not possible, forgiveness must be. The believer refuses to carry the poison. He leaves justice in God's hands. He walks free.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Ask the Holy Spirit to show you one person you have not truly forgiven.
2. Write out the debt you feel they owe you, then prayerfully cancel it and tear up the paper.
3. Pray for that person for seven days, asking God to bless them.
4. If safe and appropriate, take one step toward reconciliation this week.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Offense is the bait Satan uses to trap believers in unforgiveness, which grows into bitterness and defiles many. Forgiveness is a decision that cancels the debt and can coexist with healthy boundaries.

**Key principle:** "Let all bitterness... be put away from you... and be ye kind one to another, tenderhearted, forgiving one another, even as God for Christ's sake hath forgiven you" (Eph. 4:31-32).

**Core teaching points:**
- The Greek *skandalon* was the trigger stick in a trap; offense works the same way.
- Unforgiveness gives Satan an advantage (2 Cor. 2:10-11).
- Held offense becomes a root of bitterness that troubles and defiles many (Heb. 12:15).
- Unforgiveness costs peace, health, relationships, and spiritual authority (Matt. 18:23-35).
- Forgiveness is a decision to cancel the debt, stop rehearsing the offense, and pray for the offender.
- Reconciliation is a process that requires repentance and safety; forgiveness and boundaries can coexist.

**Real-world example:** A worker is passed over for a promotion and begins rehearsing the offense, growing sarcastic at home and cold at church. The bitterness spreads until the person chooses to cancel the debt and pray for the one who received the promotion.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Waiting until feelings change to forgive, or believing forgiveness requires immediate trust and reconciliation.

**Practice assignment:** Ask the Holy Spirit to show you one person you have not truly forgiven. Write out the debt you feel they owe, pray to cancel it, and pray for that person for seven days.

**Worksheet idea:** List offenses you are holding, the debt you demand, what canceling the debt looks like, any boundaries needed, and a prayer for the offender.

**Completion requirement:** The student writes out one debt, cancels it prayerfully, and begins a seven-day prayer commitment for the offender.

---

## Questions on Lesson 18 — The Bait of Satan: Offense, Unforgiveness, and Bitterness

- What does *skandalon* illustrate about offense?
ANSWER: It is the trigger/bait that springs a trap.
- Why does unforgiveness give Satan an advantage?
ANSWER: It provides a foothold and keeps the believer imprisoned.
- Can forgiveness and boundaries coexist?
ANSWER: Yes. Forgiveness is always required; reconciliation and trust depend on safety and repentance.



---

### Lesson 19 — The Angel of Light: Counterfeit Religion and False Revival

## 1. Counterfeit Religion Is Satan's Specialty

Satan was created for worship. Before his fall, he stood as a covering cherub in the presence of God. He knows what genuine worship, genuine prophecy, genuine miracles, and genuine doctrine look like. After his rebellion, he did not forget what he had seen. He perverted it. That is why his counterfeits can look so close to the truth.

Paul warned the Corinthians about this directly. "For such are false apostles, deceitful workers, transforming themselves into the apostles of Christ. And no marvel; for Satan himself is transformed into an angel of light. Therefore it is no great thing if his ministers also be transformed as the ministers of righteousness; whose end shall be according to their works" (2 Cor. 11:13–15). The word "transformed" means disguised. Satan dresses up his servants to look like servants of God.

This lesson is not a call to cynicism. God is doing genuine work in the earth. The Holy Spirit moves, saves, heals, prophesies, and revives. But because God is real, counterfeits exist. The more valuable the real thing, the more counterfeits appear.

## 2. How Satan Counterfeits Worship

Satan counterfeits worship by offering emotional experiences that bypass the truth. True worship is "in spirit and in truth" (John 4:24). It engages the whole person — mind, will, emotion, and spirit — under the authority of God's Word. Counterfeit worship may be highly emotional but weak on truth. It may emphasize experience over obedience. It may make people feel close to God while leaving them unchanged in holiness.

False worship also includes the worship of created things, self, angels, and spirits. Paul warned the Colossians not to be beguiled by voluntary humility and the worship of angels (Col. 2:18). Whenever worship directs attention away from Jesus Christ and toward another object, experience, or mediator, a counterfeit is present.

## 3. Counterfeit Prophecy and Miracles

The Bible records many genuine miracles and prophecies. It also records many counterfeits. Pharaoh's magicians duplicated some of Moses' signs (Ex. 7:11–12, 22). The prophets of Baal called down fire, though not from heaven (1 Kings 18). In the last days, Jesus warned, "There shall arise false Christs, and false prophets, and shall shew great signs and wonders; insomuch that, if it were possible, they shall deceive the very elect" (Matt. 24:24). Miracles are not automatic proof of truth.

A true prophetic word will agree with Scripture, exalt Christ, and produce the fruit of holiness. A false prophecy may be dramatic, flattering, or fear-based. It may claim authority that belongs only to the Bible. It may direct attention to the prophet rather than to God.

John gave a simple test: "Beloved, believe not every spirit, but try the spirits whether they are of God: because many false prophets are gone out into the world. Hereby know ye the Spirit of God: Every spirit that confesseth that Jesus Christ is come in the flesh is of God: and every spirit that confesseth not that Jesus Christ is come in the flesh is not of God" (1 John 4:1–3). The test is doctrinal, not merely experiential.

## 4. Counterfeit Doctrine

Satan's most dangerous counterfeits are doctrinal because doctrine shapes everything else. A small error in doctrine can produce a large error in practice. Paul warned Timothy that the Spirit expressly said that in latter times some would depart from the faith, giving heed to seducing spirits and doctrines of demons (1 Tim. 4:1). Demons teach through human mouths. They do not appear with horns. They appear with Bible verses taken out of context.

False doctrine usually does one of three things. It subtracts from the person or work of Christ. It adds to the finished work of Christ. Or it redirects the believer's confidence from Christ to something else — a ritual, a leader, a method, or an experience.

The Bereans in Acts 17:11 set the pattern. They received the word with readiness, but they searched the Scriptures daily to see whether the things taught were so. Even the teaching of the apostle Paul was tested against Scripture.

## 5. False Revival vs. Genuine Move of God

Revival is real. When God moves, lives are transformed, churches are awakened, and society is affected. But not every movement that calls itself revival is from God. Some are emotional waves. Some are personality cults. Some are demonic deceptions.

A genuine move of God produces lasting fruit. It deepens love for Scripture, increases holiness, restores families, stirs prayer, and exalts Jesus Christ. A counterfeit movement may produce excitement without transformation. It may produce miracles without morality. It may produce numbers without depth. It may produce a following for a man rather than devotion to Christ.

The fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, longsuffering, gentleness, goodness, faith, meekness, and self-control (Gal. 5:22–23). The works of the flesh are manifest and include hatred, variance, emulations, wrath, strife, seditions, heresies, and envyings (Gal. 5:19–21). A movement can claim the Holy Spirit, but its fruit will reveal its source.

## 6. The Tests: Fruit and Doctrine

Jesus gave the test of fruit in the Sermon on the Mount. "Beware of false prophets, which come to you in sheep's clothing, but inwardly they are ravening wolves. Ye shall know them by their fruits" (Matt. 7:15–16). Good trees produce good fruit. Bad trees produce bad fruit. The fruit is not the size of the crowd or the number of miracles. The fruit is the character produced in people over time.

Doctrine is the other test. Jesus said, "If any man will do his will, he shall know of the doctrine, whether it be of God, or whether I speak of myself" (John 7:17). Willingness to obey God opens the door to discernment. A person who wants the truth will find it. A person who wants an experience will often be deceived.

Both tests work together. Fruit without doctrine can become sentimental. Doctrine without fruit can become dead orthodoxy. The genuine move of God produces both sound doctrine and godly character.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Examine one spiritual movement or teacher you follow. Test it by fruit and by doctrine.
2. Read Acts 17:11 and commit to searching the Scriptures daily.
3. Memorize 1 John 4:1 and use it as a filter for every spiritual claim.
4. Ask the Holy Spirit to increase your love for the truth, not merely your desire for experience.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Satan counterfeits worship, prophecy, miracles, doctrine, and revival because he once stood in God's presence. A genuine move of God produces both sound doctrine and godly fruit.

**Key principle:** "Beloved, believe not every spirit, but try the spirits whether they are of God... Every spirit that confesseth that Jesus Christ is come in the flesh is of God" (1 John 4:1-3).

**Core teaching points:**
- Satan transforms himself into an angel of light and his ministers as ministers of righteousness (2 Cor. 11:13-15).
- Counterfeit worship is emotional experience without truth and obedience (John 4:24).
- Miracles and prophecy are not automatic proof of truth; they must be tested by Scripture (Matt. 24:24; 1 John 4:1-3).
- False doctrine usually subtracts from Christ, adds to His finished work, or redirects confidence elsewhere.
- The Bereans tested even the apostle Paul by searching the Scriptures daily (Acts 17:11).
- Genuine revival produces love for Scripture, holiness, prayer, restored relationships, and exaltation of Christ.

**Real-world example:** A person joins a movement with dramatic experiences and miracles but notices members neglect Scripture, family, and holiness. The person learns to test the movement by fruit and doctrine rather than emotion.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Accepting spiritual experiences, signs, or popularity as automatic evidence that God is behind a teacher or movement.

**Practice assignment:** Examine one spiritual movement or teacher you follow this week, testing it by fruit and doctrine. Commit to searching the Scriptures daily.

**Worksheet idea:** Evaluate one teacher or movement: list claimed experiences, doctrine about Christ, fruit in character and relationships, alignment with Scripture, and your conclusion.

**Completion requirement:** The student completes a written evaluation of one spiritual influence and commits to daily Scripture searching for the next week.

---

## Questions on Lesson 19 — The Angel of Light: Counterfeit Religion and False Revival

- What two tests reveal whether a movement is from God?
ANSWER: Fruit and doctrine.
- Why did the Bereans search the Scriptures daily?
ANSWER: To test even apostolic teaching against God's Word.
- According to 1 John 4:1-3, what doctrinal test must a true spirit pass?
ANSWER: It must confess that Jesus Christ has come in the flesh.



---

### Lesson 20 — Reading Satan's Mind: Patterns You Can Learn to Recognize

## 1. Why Patterns Matter

Satan is not infinitely creative. He is cunning, but he works from a limited playbook. The more you study that playbook, the less often he can surprise you. Paul told the Corinthians, "We are not ignorant of his devices" (2 Cor. 2:11). The word "devices" means schemes, methods, or stratagems. Ignorance of these schemes makes the believer vulnerable. Knowledge of them makes the believer alert.

This lesson synthesizes biblical patterns with the practical observation that Satan exploits asymmetries. He knows things about you that you may not know about yourself. He observes your habits, your weaknesses, your timing, and your pressures. He uses that knowledge to attack at the moment of maximum vulnerability. Learning to recognize these patterns is part of becoming spiritually mature.

## 2. The Knowledge-Asymmetry Principle

Satan has been watching human beings for thousands of years. He knows how people fall. He knows which temptations work at which seasons. He knows how to dress up a lie so that it looks like wisdom. This gives him a knowledge advantage over the untaught believer.

The counter to this asymmetry is truth. The believer who knows Scripture, knows his own weaknesses, and knows the patterns of the enemy can level the field. Proverbs 24:5 says, "A wise man is strong; yea, a man of knowledge increaseth strength." Knowledge of the truth is strength.

The knowledge-asymmetry principle means Satan attacks where you are uninformed, unprepared, or overconfident. His questions always target a gap in your understanding. The young believer is attacked on assurance. The proud believer is attacked on self-sufficiency. The wounded believer is attacked on identity. The busy believer is attacked on neglect.

## 3. Exploiting Rugged Individualism

One of Satan's favorite entry points is rugged individualism. He whispers, "You do not need anyone else. You can figure this out on your own. No one understands you like you understand yourself." This lie appeals to pride, but it isolates the believer from protection.

The Bible places the believer in a body. Hebrews 10:24–25 commands us to consider one another, to provoke one another to love and good works, and not to forsake the assembling of ourselves together. Proverbs says there is safety in a multitude of counselors (Prov. 11:14). The person who will not be corrected is heading for a fall (Prov. 29:1).

Satan loves the lone ranger because the lone ranger has no one to warn him. He can be deceived for a long time without anyone noticing. The antidote is to stay connected to the church, to mentors, and to accountable relationships.

## 4. Half-Truths and Twisted Scripture

Satan rarely tells a lie that is entirely false. He prefers a half-truth. A half-truth has the appearance of wisdom and is harder to refute. In the wilderness, Satan quoted Psalm 91 to Jesus but left out the condition of dwelling in the secret place of the Most High (Matt. 4:6; Ps. 91:1, 11–12). The verse was real. The application was false.

A half-truth often contains a real fact, a real feeling, or a real Bible verse, but it draws a false conclusion. "God wants you to be happy" is a half-truth. God does want joy for His people, but He does not justify sin to produce it. "God has not given you a spirit of fear" is true (2 Tim. 1:7), but it does not mean every fear is demonic. Some fear is wisdom.

The way to detect a half-truth is to ask three questions. What part of this is true? What part is being left out? Where is this leading me? If the destination is away from obedience, away from trust in God, or away from the local church, the half-truth is bait.

## 5. Urgency, Isolation, and Weariness

Satan often attacks when you are tired, alone, or in transition. These three conditions lower your spiritual defenses.

**Weariness.** Elijah after Mount Carmel was exhausted, and he asked God to take his life (1 Kings 19:4). The great victory was followed by the great collapse because he was physically and emotionally spent. Satan knows that a tired believer makes poor decisions. That is why rest, Sabbath, and physical care are spiritual disciplines.

**Isolation.** Jesus warned the disciples in Gethsemane to watch and pray, but they slept while He prayed alone. When the pressure came, they scattered. Isolation makes every temptation stronger because there is no one to call you back. The person who cuts off fellowship becomes easy prey.

**Transition.** Major changes — new job, new city, new marriage, new baby, new ministry, grief, or promotion — create instability. Satan attacks in transition because routines are broken, support systems are thin, and identity feels uncertain. The believer in transition must be extra vigilant.

## 6. Building a Personal Warning System

You can build a personal warning system against Satan's patterns. This system is not complicated, but it must be maintained.

First, know your triggers. What situations, emotions, or people most often precede your spiritual decline? Write them down. Second, know your escape routes. If you are tempted when tired, go to bed instead of scrolling. If you are tempted when alone, call a believer. If you are tempted in transition, increase your time in Scripture and prayer. Third, keep short accounts with God. Confess sin quickly. Do not let the enemy build a case. Fourth, review your life regularly. Ask, "Where have I been most attacked? What patterns do I see?"

The goal is not to become obsessed with Satan. The goal is to become hard to fool. A wise believer learns from his own history and from the history of others in Scripture.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Write down three times you have been spiritually attacked. Identify any common conditions such as weariness, isolation, or transition.
2. Choose one trigger and create a concrete escape plan.
3. Memorize 2 Corinthians 2:11: "We are not ignorant of his devices."
4. This week, tell a trusted believer one pattern you have noticed and ask them to help you watch for it.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Satan is cunning but not infinitely creative. Believers become hard to fool by studying his schemes, knowing their own triggers, and building a personal warning system rooted in Scripture and accountability.

**Key principle:** "We are not ignorant of his devices" (2 Cor. 2:11). Knowledge of the truth turns vulnerability into strength.

**Core teaching points:**
- Satan works from a limited playbook of schemes or devices (2 Cor. 2:11).
- He exploits knowledge asymmetry, attacking where a believer is uninformed, unprepared, or overconfident.
- Rugged individualism isolates the believer from protection and counsel.
- Half-truths and twisted Scripture are more dangerous than obvious lies.
- He often attacks during weariness, isolation, or transition.
- A personal warning system includes knowing triggers, escape routes, quick confession, and regular review.

**Real-world example:** After moving to a new city, a believer stops attending church, becomes lonely, and falls back into an old habit. The believer later recognizes the pattern: transition plus isolation lowered defenses, and the answer is to reconnect quickly with a local church.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Assuming maturity makes one immune to deception, or ignoring physical and emotional conditions that lower spiritual defenses.

**Practice assignment:** Write down three times you have been spiritually attacked, identify any common conditions such as weariness, isolation, or transition, and create one concrete escape plan.

**Worksheet idea:** Create a personal pattern map with columns: trigger condition, lie Satan uses, Scripture response, practical escape route, and the believer you will tell.

**Completion requirement:** The student identifies one recurring trigger pattern, shares it with a trusted believer, and begins using the escape plan.

---

## Questions on Lesson 20 — Reading Satan's Mind: Patterns You Can Learn to Recognize

- What does "devices" in 2 Corinthians 2:11 mean?
ANSWER: Schemes, methods, or stratagems.
- Why is rugged individualism a spiritual vulnerability?
ANSWER: It removes counselors and accountability that would expose deception early.
- What three questions expose a half-truth?
ANSWER: What part is true? What is left out? Where is it leading me?



---

### Lesson 21 — Satan Considering the Saints: How He Studies You

## 1. Satan Is a Student of the Saints

Satan is not random in his attacks. He studies the people of God. The old preachers used to say that the devil has a file on every believer. That picture is helpful. He pays attention to your habits, your temperament, your history, your relationships, your health, your finances, and your spiritual condition. He watches when you rise and when you fall. He notices what makes you discouraged and what makes you proud.

This is not paranoia. The Bible presents Satan as an active observer. In Job 1:7, Satan answered the Lord, "From going to and fro in the earth, and from walking up and down in it." He was watching. In Job 2, he returned and made another accusation based on what he had seen. Peter said the devil walks about as a roaring lion, seeking whom he may devour (1 Pet. 5:8). The word "seeking" implies observation and selection. Jesus told Peter, "Simon, Simon, behold, Satan hath desired to have you, that he may sift you as wheat: but I have prayed for thee, that thy faith fail not" (Luke 22:31–32). Satan had studied Peter. He knew Peter's weakness was self-confidence.

The good news is that Satan's study is limited. He is not omniscient. He is a creature. He can only know what he observes or what he infers. More importantly, God's knowledge of you is greater than Satan's, and God's purpose for you will prevail.

## 2. He Studies Your Infirmities

One of the first things Satan notices is your infirmities. The word includes physical weakness, emotional wounds, mental struggles, and any area of brokenness. Paul wrote about a "thorn in the flesh," which he called "the messenger of Satan" (2 Cor. 12:7). This affliction was allowed by God for a purpose, but it was also an area where Satan could buffet Paul.

Your infirmity may be a chronic condition, a past trauma, a family wound, a tendency toward anxiety, or a besetting sin. Satan will try to use it to make you doubt God's love, to isolate you, or to drive you to false comfort. The person with a history of depression may be attacked with despair. The person with a physical limitation may be attacked with self-pity.

The answer is not denial of the infirmity but reliance on grace. Paul prayed three times for the thorn to be removed, and God answered, "My grace is sufficient for thee: for my strength is made perfect in weakness" (2 Cor. 12:9). Satan studies your weakness so he can exploit it. God allows your weakness so He can display His strength.

## 3. He Studies Your Moods

Satan also watches your emotional state. He knows when you are elated, depressed, lonely, angry, bored, or anxious. Each mood presents a different temptation. When you are elated, he tempts you to pride. When you are depressed, he tempts you to despair. When you are lonely, he tempts you to false intimacy. When you are angry, he tempts you to retaliation. When you are bored, he temptes you to stimulation.

The life of David shows this pattern. When David was at rest in his palace, he fell into adultery with Bathsheba (2 Sam. 11). When he was fleeing from Absalom, he was attacked with despair. When he was victorious, he sometimes became proud. Satan knew David's moods and tailored his temptations accordingly.

The believer who understands this will guard his emotional life. He will not make major decisions in a mood. He will learn to bring every feeling to God and let truth interpret it. Psalm 42 shows the model: the psalmist talked to his own soul and directed it to hope in God.

## 4. He Studies Your Position

Satan studies your position in life. Your job, your influence, your family role, your finances, your reputation, and your responsibilities all present openings. The person with money is tempted to trust it. The person with authority is tempted to abuse it. The person with a platform is tempted to love praise. The person with a hidden life is tempted to think no one sees.

The temptations of Jesus in the wilderness were related to His position as the Messiah. Satan offered Him bread because He was hungry, spectacle because He had divine power, and kingdoms because He was the rightful King. The attacks were tailored to who Jesus was.

Your position is not the problem. It is the battlefield. God has placed you where you are for His purposes. Satan wants to use that placement to corrupt you or stop you. The believer must steward position with humility and accountability.

## 5. He Studies Your Affections

Satan studies what you love. Your affections are the deepest part of your motivation. He knows what you desire, what you fear losing, and what you will protect at any cost. This is why 1 John 2:15 warns, "Love not the world, neither the things that are in the world. If any man love the world, the love of the Father is not in him."

Your affections may include family, success, comfort, recognition, knowledge, beauty, or ministry. None of these are evil in themselves. But when any affection becomes greater than your affection for God, it becomes an idol. Satan will pressure that idol because he knows it controls you.

The antidote is to keep your first love alive. Jesus told the church at Ephesus that they had left their first love and must return to their first works (Rev. 2:4–5). When Christ is your supreme affection, Satan cannot hold you hostage with lesser loves.

## 6. God's Protective Limits and Greater Purpose

Satan studies you, but he cannot do whatever he wants. The story of Job is the clearest proof. Satan could not touch Job without God's permission. God set the boundaries. Satan could afflict Job's body, his family, and his possessions, but he could not take Job's life.

This truth should produce both humility and confidence. Humility, because we are not so strong that Satan cannot touch us. Confidence, because nothing touches us that God has not allowed. Romans 8:28 promises that all things work together for good to those who love God and are called according to His purpose. Satan's attacks are real, but they are inside God's plan.

God's purpose is greater than Satan's malice. Satan desired to sift Peter, but Jesus prayed that Peter's faith would not fail. The sifting produced a humbled, useful apostle. Paul had a messenger of Satan, but it kept him from pride and made him depend on grace. Your trials may be used by Satan to destroy you, but God will use them to refine you if you trust Him.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Write down your known infirmities, common moods, current position, and deepest affections. Ask God to show you where Satan is most likely to attack.
2. Read Job 1–2 and underline every boundary God placed on Satan.
3. Memorize Luke 22:32 and thank Jesus that He prays for you when you are sifted.
4. Choose one area of weakness and ask a mature believer to pray with you for strength and watchfulness.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Satan studies believers' infirmities, moods, positions, and affections to tailor his attacks, but he is a limited creature who operates within boundaries God sets. God's knowledge, purpose, and intercession are greater than Satan's malice.

**Key principle:** "My grace is sufficient for thee: for my strength is made perfect in weakness" (2 Cor. 12:9). God allows weakness so His strength can be displayed.

**Core teaching points:**
- Satan is an active observer; he walks about seeking whom he may devour (1 Pet. 5:8; Job 1:7).
- He studies infirmities such as physical weakness, emotional wounds, and besetting sins (2 Cor. 12:7).
- He studies moods, because each emotional state presents a different temptation.
- He studies position: money, authority, platform, and hidden life all present openings.
- He studies affections and pressures idols because he knows what controls the heart.
- Satan is not omniscient and cannot act beyond limits God permits (Job 1-2).
- God uses the very trials Satan intends for destruction to refine and strengthen faith.

**Real-world example:** A leader notices that after every major success he becomes proud and short with his team. He recognizes that Satan studies his position and moods, so he builds accountability and thanksgiving rituals around wins.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Hiding weaknesses, positions, and affections from God and community, pretending Satan cannot exploit them.

**Practice assignment:** Write down your known infirmities, common moods, current position, and deepest affections. Ask God and one mature believer where Satan is most likely to attack you.

**Worksheet idea:** Make a four-column inventory: infirmity/mood/position/affection; the lie Satan may use; God's grace and purpose in that area; the Scripture and accountability step you will take.

**Completion requirement:** The student completes the personal inventory and asks one mature believer to pray for watchfulness in one identified area.

---

## Questions on Lesson 21 — Satan Considering the Saints: How He Studies You

- Is Satan omniscient?
ANSWER: No. He is a creature who can only observe and infer.
- What did Paul call his affliction in 2 Corinthians 12:7?
ANSWER: A thorn in the flesh, the messenger of Satan.
- Why does God allow weakness in a believer's life?
ANSWER: To display His strength and keep the believer from pride.



---

### Lesson 22 — The Deadly Trio: Jezebel, Witchcraft, and the Religious Spirit

## 1. The Deadly Trio Defined

Scripture speaks of specific evil influences that operate in relationships, churches, and movements. This lesson examines three that often work together: the Jezebel spirit, the spirit of witchcraft, and the religious spirit. These are not three separate demons in a formal hierarchy. They are three patterns of demonic operation that share common goals: control, domination, and the suppression of genuine spiritual life.

The Bible gives us the historical figures behind these patterns. Jezebel was the Phoenician queen who promoted Baal worship, persecuted the prophets of God, and controlled Israel through her husband Ahab (1 Kings 16–21). Witchcraft in Scripture includes both occult practice and the sinful desire to control people and outcomes through forbidden means (1 Sam. 15:23). The religious spirit finds its clearest expression in the Pharisees, who had the form of godliness but denied the power thereof (2 Tim. 3:5; Matt. 23).

Together these three form a deadly trio. Jezebel seduces and controls. Witchcraft intimidates and dominates. The religious spirit legalizes and deadens. They often appear in the same person, the same church, or the same season of attack.

## 2. The Jezebel Spirit

The Jezebel spirit is characterized by control, manipulation, and seduction. It wants to rule. It uses flattery, fear, or sexual and emotional appeal to gain influence. It especially targets leadership. Elijah stood against Jezebel's prophets on Mount Carmel, but afterward Jezebel sent a message threatening his life, and the great prophet ran in fear (1 Kings 19:1–3). That shows the intimidation power of this influence.

In a church, the Jezebel spirit may operate through a person who claims special spiritual authority, prophetic insight, or intimate knowledge of the pastor's needs. Such a person may resist accountability, sow division, and demand loyalty. The church in Thyatira was rebuked for tolerating "that woman Jezebel, which calleth herself a prophetess, to teach and to seduce my servants to commit fornication, and to eat things sacrificed unto idols" (Rev. 2:20). Jesus said He would judge her and her children.

The Jezebel spirit seduces by offering what people want: recognition, spiritual experiences, approval, or a sense of being special. It controls by making people afraid to disagree. The antidote is godly authority that refuses to be manipulated, transparent leadership, and a church culture where every prophetic word is tested.

## 3. The Spirit of Witchcraft

Witchcraft in Scripture has a broader meaning than modern occult practice. Samuel told Saul, "Rebellion is as the sin of witchcraft, and stubbornness is as iniquity and idolatry" (1 Sam. 15:23). Witchcraft is the attempt to control people, circumstances, or the future through forbidden power. It is the refusal to submit to God's sovereignty.

The spirit of witchcraft operates through domination, intimidation, and manipulation. It wants to force outcomes. It uses pressure, threats, guilt, and spiritual claims to make people do what it wants. It may appear in occult forms such as spells, curses, and divination. It may also appear in ordinary relationships where one person seeks to control another through fear or emotional pressure.

The believer's protection against witchcraft is submission to God. James 4:7 says, "Submit yourselves therefore to God. Resist the devil, and he will flee from you." Witchcraft loses its power when a person refuses to be controlled by fear and instead stands in the authority of Christ. The blood of Jesus, the name of Jesus, and the Word of God break the intimidation.

## 4. The Religious Spirit

The religious spirit is perhaps the most deceptive of the three because it looks so much like Christianity. It loves ritual, rules, reputation, and religious performance. It majors on externals and minors on the heart. It produces people who are clean on the outside but full of dead men's bones on the inside, as Jesus said of the Pharisees (Matt. 23:27).

The religious spirit creates legalism. It adds human traditions to God's commands and treats those traditions as equal to Scripture. It produces dead works — activity that looks spiritual but has no life. It resists the freedom of the Spirit. It persecutes those who walk in grace. It cannot recognize the work of God if it comes outside its own system.

The religious spirit also loves control. It uses shame, exclusion, and doctrinal nitpicking to keep people in line. It makes Christianity a performance rather than a relationship. Paul wrote that the letter kills but the Spirit gives life (2 Cor. 3:6). The religious spirit is the letter without the Spirit.

## 5. How the Three Work Together

These three spirits often work together to destroy a church or a life. The Jezebel spirit seduces people away from truth. The witchcraft spirit intimidates those who resist. The religious spirit legitimizes the whole operation with a veneer of piety.

Consider how this trio can affect a local church. A controlling leader rises to influence through charisma and flattery (Jezebel). Those who question the leader are silenced through fear, gossip, or claims of spiritual authority (witchcraft). The whole thing is defended as a necessary move of God, a new standard of holiness, or a return to the old ways (religious spirit). By the time people see the damage, many have already been hurt.

The same trio can operate in a family. A parent uses emotional appeal to control children (Jezebel), punishes dissent (witchcraft), and justifies it all as biblical authority (religious spirit). The children grow up confused about God, grace, and love.

## 6. Recognizing and Resisting the Trio

Recognizing these spirits requires discernment and courage. Here are the signs to watch for.

A Jezebel influence brings control through seduction or intimidation, refuses accountability, claims special revelation, and sows division. A witchcraft influence brings pressure, fear, curses, manipulation, and the sense that you must do something or something bad will happen. A religious influence brings legalism, dead ritual, pride in performance, judgment of others, and resistance to the moving of the Holy Spirit.

To resist them, begin with your own heart. Are you trying to control others? Are you using religious language to justify manipulation? Are you more concerned with being right than with being loving? Repent first.

Then stand in truth. Jesus said, "Ye shall know the truth, and the truth shall make you free" (John 8:32). Expose control by speaking the truth in love (Eph. 4:15). Refuse to participate in manipulation. Maintain healthy boundaries. Stay accountable to mature leaders who are not under the same influence.

Finally, pray. These influences are spiritual, and they require spiritual resistance. Put on the whole armor of God. Resist the devil. He will flee.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Ask the Holy Spirit to show you whether any of these three influences are present in your relationships or church.
2. Examine your own heart for control, manipulation, or legalism. Confess what you find.
3. Read Revelation 2:18–29 and ask God for the courage to confront Jezebel-like control with grace.
4. Commit to being a person who walks in the Spirit, speaks the truth in love, and refuses every form of spiritual control.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Jezebel, witchcraft, and the religious spirit are three patterns of demonic control that suppress genuine spiritual life. Believers resist them by first examining their own hearts, then standing in truth, maintaining boundaries, and using spiritual authority.

**Key principle:** "Submit yourselves therefore to God. Resist the devil, and he will flee from you" (James 4:7). Truth and submission to God break the power of control.

**Core teaching points:**
- These three are patterns of operation, not a formal hierarchy of demons.
- The Jezebel spirit controls through seduction, flattery, fear, and false spiritual authority (Rev. 2:20).
- The spirit of witchcraft controls people and outcomes through domination, intimidation, and forbidden power (1 Sam. 15:23; James 4:7).
- The religious spirit legalizes, deadens, and majors on externals while missing the heart (Matt. 23; 2 Tim. 3:5).
- The three often work together: Jezebel seduces, witchcraft intimidates, and the religious spirit legitimizes.
- Recognition begins with self-examination for control, manipulation, or legalism in one's own heart.
- Resistance comes through submission to God, speaking truth in love, healthy boundaries, and the armor of God.

**Real-world example:** A church member notices a leader rising through charisma and silencing dissent with fear, defended as a necessary return to holiness. The member first examines his own heart, then speaks truth in love, sets boundaries, and stays accountable to mature elders.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Labeling other people as Jezebel, witchcraft, or religious without first repenting of control, manipulation, or legalism in one's own heart.

**Practice assignment:** Ask the Holy Spirit to show you whether any of these three influences are present in your relationships or church. Examine your own heart and read Revelation 2:18-29.

**Worksheet idea:** Self-examination grid: signs of Jezebel, witchcraft, and religious spirit in my own life; signs in my environment; the Scripture response for each; the boundary or truth-speaking step I will take.

**Completion requirement:** The student completes the self-examination, confesses any matching personal patterns, and identifies one boundary or truth-speaking step to take.

## Questions on Lesson 22 — The Deadly Trio: Jezebel, Witchcraft, and the Religious Spirit

- What three common goals unite the deadly trio?
ANSWER: Control, domination, and the suppression of genuine spiritual life.
- How does witchcraft appear beyond occult rituals?
ANSWER: Through rebellion, stubbornness, intimidation, and the attempt to force outcomes.
- What is the first step in resisting these influences?
ANSWER: Examine and repent of one's own control, manipulation, or legalism.



---

## Part IV — Satan's Systems: Occult, Ideology, and World Structures

### Lesson 23 — Occult Deception: Witchcraft, Wicca, and the New Age

## 1. Why the Occult Is Not Harmless

The occult is not a hobby, a costume, or a harmless cultural curiosity. It is a system of spiritual deception that opens a person to demonic influence and leads the soul away from the living God. The church has too often treated occult involvement as entertainment, as if tarot cards, astrology, séances, and spirit guides were nothing more than imaginative games. Scripture treats them differently. Deuteronomy 18:10–12 calls such practices an abomination to the Lord. Galatians 5:20 lists witchcraft among the works of the flesh that will bar a person from the kingdom of God. Revelation 21:8 places sorcerers in the lake of fire. These are not minor matters.

The word "occult" means hidden or secret. The occult offers hidden knowledge, hidden power, and hidden contact with spiritual beings. That offer is the same offer Satan made to Eve in Eden: secret wisdom that bypasses God's authority. "Ye shall be as gods" (Gen. 3:5). Every occult system promises the same thing. It promises power without obedience, knowledge without revelation, and spiritual experience without submission to Jesus Christ. That promise is a lie, and the one telling it has not changed in six thousand years.

This lesson examines three related forms of occult deception: witchcraft, Wicca, and the New Age movement. They differ in style and packaging, but they share a common source. They are Satan's counterfeits of true spirituality. They appeal to the human desire for meaning, mystery, healing, and power. They must be answered with the truth of the gospel and the authority of Christ.

## 2. Witchcraft and Wicca: Rebranded Rebellion

Witchcraft is an ancient practice that appears throughout the Bible. Moses confronted it in Egypt. Samuel confronted it at Endor. Elijah confronted it on Mount Carmel. The Law of Moses demanded the death penalty for witches and mediums, not because Israel was primitive, but because witchcraft is rebellion against the one true God (Ex. 22:18; Lev. 20:6, 27; Deut. 18:10–12).

Wicca, which emerged in the twentieth century, presents itself as a benign nature religion. Its practitioners often say they honor the goddess, harm none, and celebrate the cycles of the earth. The language is gentle, ecological, and feminist. But underneath the rebranding is the same old rebellion. Wicca teaches that divinity is within nature, within the self, and within the cycle of life and death. It does not call for repentance before a holy God. It does not acknowledge sin, judgment, or the need for a Savior. It offers a spirituality without the cross.

The apostle Paul wrote that the things the Gentiles sacrifice, they sacrifice to devils and not to God (1 Cor. 10:20). That principle has not changed. When a person invokes spirits, casts spells, draws down the moon, or seeks guidance from unseen entities, the spirits that answer are not neutral. They are demonic. The names may sound beautiful — Diana, Hecate, Pan, the Great Mother — but the reality behind the names is the same ancient kingdom of darkness.

The book of Acts records the conversion of a sorcerer named Elymas, who tried to turn a Roman official away from the faith. Paul, filled with the Holy Ghost, rebuked him and struck him blind (Acts 13:6–12). The incident shows that true spiritual authority belongs to Christ and His apostles, not to those who manipulate unseen forces. It also shows that occult power is real enough to be dangerous, but not real enough to stand against the name of Jesus.

## 3. The New Age: Ancient Lies in Modern Packaging

The New Age movement is the occult dressed in Western, middle-class, self-help language. It speaks of energy, vibrations, higher consciousness, and the law of attraction. It offers healing through crystals, chakras, reiki, and channeling. It borrows from Eastern mysticism, Native American spirituality, and Western esotericism. It is eclectic, which means it is hard to pin down, but its core message is consistent: you are divine, reality is an illusion, and you can create your own truth.

This is the oldest lie in the world. Satan told Eve, "Ye shall be as gods" (Gen. 3:5). The New Age tells the same lie in a thousand forms. It tells you that you are a spark of the divine. It tells you that sin is an illusion created by low vibration. It tells you that Jesus was one of many ascended masters, not the only Savior and Lord. Every one of these claims is a denial of the gospel.

The New Age is also a rebellion against the doctrine of creation. Romans 1:25 describes those who change the truth of God into a lie and worship and serve the creature more than the Creator. The New Age worships the creation, the self, and the cosmic energy. It does not worship the Creator who made all things and who alone is worthy of worship. When a Christian dabbles in yoga as purely physical exercise, meditation as emptying the mind, or reiki as harmless energy work, he must ask whether he is borrowing from a system that denies Christ.

The book of Colossians warns the church not to be spoiled through philosophy and vain deceit, after the tradition of men, after the rudiments of the world, and not after Christ (Col. 2:8). The New Age is exactly that: rudiments of the world, traditions of men, and vain deceit. It does not save. It binds.

## 4. The Bible's Judgment on Occult Practices

The Bible is not silent about the occult. It is severe. Deuteronomy 18:10–12 lists the abominations: one who causes his son or daughter to pass through the fire, a diviner, an observer of times, an enchanter, a witch, a charmer, a consulter with familiar spirits, a wizard, or a necromancer. Every one of these practices is an abomination to the Lord. The reason is not arbitrary. These practices represent a turning away from God's revelation to demonic counterfeits.

In Acts 19, the new believers in Ephesus brought their occult books and burned them in public. The value of the books was fifty thousand pieces of silver. The repentance was costly, but it was total. They did not keep a few scrolls for research or sentimental reasons. They burned them because they understood that these objects carried spiritual contamination. That is the biblical pattern. Occult involvement is not managed. It is renounced.

Revelation 18:23 says that the great city Babylon was deceived by sorceries. The Greek word for sorceries is *pharmakeia*, from which we get the English word pharmacy. The connection is not accidental. Sorcery in the ancient world often used drugs and potions to induce altered states. The modern revival of psychedelics, plant medicine, and shamanic ceremonies is a return to the same pharmakeia. The church must not be naive. When the state legalizes or celebrates such practices, the spiritual effect is the same: doors are opened to deception.

## 5. The Doorway: Curiosity, Pain, and Power

Why do people enter the occult? Three doors are common. The first is curiosity. A person reads a horoscope, plays with a Ouija board, or watches a popular show about witches. The curiosity seems innocent, but it becomes an invitation. The second is pain. A grieving person goes to a medium to contact a dead loved one. A lonely person seeks a spirit guide for comfort. A sick person turns to energy healing when medicine fails. The occult preys on human suffering. The third is power. A person wants influence, knowledge, revenge, or success, and the occult offers a shortcut.

The church must answer all three doors. We answer curiosity with teaching. We answer pain with the compassion of Christ and the community of the saints. We answer the desire for power with the far greater power of the Holy Spirit, given to those who obey God. The occult offers a counterfeit of each of these. It offers teaching, but it is false teaching. It offers comfort, but it is the comfort of a lie. It offers power, but it is the power of bondage.

Jesus said, "The thief cometh not, but for to steal, and to kill, and to destroy: I am come that they might have life, and that they might have it more abundantly" (John 10:10). The occult thief promises life but brings death. Jesus offers life that cannot be stolen.

## 6. Repentance and Renunciation

The only way out of occult involvement is the way out of every sin: repentance, confession, renunciation, and faith in Christ. Repentance means turning away from the occult completely. Confession means naming what was done before God. Renunciation means verbally breaking every agreement, vow, invitation, or submission made to demonic powers. Faith means trusting that the blood of Jesus cleanses from all sin and that Christ has already disarmed every principality and power (Col. 2:15).

A person with a history of occult involvement may need deliverance ministry. The church should not be afraid of this. Jesus cast out demons, and He gave authority to His church to do the same. At the same time, deliverance is not a magic ritual. It is the application of Christ's victory to a life that is being surrendered to Him. Without repentance and ongoing obedience, deliverance becomes temporary. With repentance and obedience, it becomes permanent.

First John 1:9 is the anchor: "If we confess our sins, he is faithful and just to forgive us our sins, and to cleanse us from all unrighteousness." The blood of Jesus is sufficient for the deepest occult defilement. No witch, no warlock, no New Age teacher, and no ancestral curse is beyond the reach of Calvary.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Search your home for occult objects, books, symbols, or media. Remove them completely, as the Ephesians burned their scrolls in Acts 19.
2. If you have ever participated in witchcraft, Wicca, the New Age, astrology, mediumship, or occult meditation, confess it to God and renounce every vow or agreement made to demonic powers.
3. Learn the names and claims of popular New Age practices so you can recognize them and answer them with Scripture.
4. Find one person this month who is flirting with the occult, and share with them that Jesus Christ is the true source of power, healing, and peace.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** The occult is not harmless entertainment; it is a system of spiritual deception that promises hidden knowledge and power while opening the soul to demonic influence. Christ alone has authority over the unseen realm, and repentance is the path out of every occult bondage.

**Key principle:** "If we confess our sins, he is faithful and just to forgive us our sins, and to cleanse us from all unrighteousness" (1 John 1:9); the blood of Jesus is sufficient for the deepest occult defilement.

**Core teaching points:**
- The occult offers the same lie Satan offered Eve: secret wisdom and power that bypasses submission to God.
- Witchcraft, Wicca, and the New Age differ in packaging but share a demonic source and deny the cross of Christ.
- Scripture is severe toward occult practices, listing divination, mediumship, sorcery, and enchantment as abominations.
- People enter the occult through curiosity, pain, or a desire for power; the church must answer each door with truth, compassion, and the Holy Spirit.
- Repentance, confession, renunciation, and faith in Christ are sufficient to cleanse every form of occult defilement.

**Real-world example:** A grieving widow visits a medium hoping to speak to her deceased husband, then begins using tarot apps and astrology for daily guidance, unaware that each contact is an invitation to a spirit that is not neutral.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Treating occult objects or practices as cultural curiosities, research material, or harmless fun instead of recognizing them as spiritually contaminated and renouncing them completely.

**Practice assignment:** Search your home, phone, and media accounts for occult objects, books, symbols, apps, or subscriptions; remove and renounce them, asking God to cleanse the space.

**Worksheet idea:** Write a three-column inventory: occult practices or objects you have encountered; the lie each one promised; and the corresponding truth of Christ that answers it.

**Completion requirement:** The student identifies and removes at least one occult influence from their life and, if applicable, verbally renounces every vow or agreement made to demonic powers under the authority of Christ.

---

## Questions on Lesson 23 — Occult Deception: Witchcraft, Wicca, and the New Age

- What lie does every occult system repeat from Eden?
ANSWER: Answer: "You can be like God" or "You can have secret wisdom and power without submitting to God."
- Why does the New Age deny the gospel?
ANSWER: Answer: It teaches that the self is divine, sin is illusion, and Jesus is merely one of many spiritual teachers rather than the only Savior and Lord.
- What did the Ephesian believers do with their occult books?
ANSWER: Answer: They brought them together and burned them publicly, at great financial cost, as an act of total repentance.



---

### Lesson 24 — Satanism, Ritual Abuse, and the Occult Underground

## 1. The Occult Underground Is Real

There exists beneath the surface of respectable society a world of organized occult activity that most people never see. It includes Satanic groups, secret societies, drug networks, human trafficking rings, and ritual practices that are deliberately hidden. Some of this activity is criminal. Some of it is spiritual. Often it is both. The church must neither deny its existence nor become obsessed with it. We must acknowledge it, pray against it, rescue the victims, and proclaim the gospel that breaks every chain.

The Bible is not surprised by hidden evil. Isaiah 29:15 says, "Woe unto them that seek deep to hide their counsel from the Lord, and their works are in the dark." Proverbs 28:1 says the wicked flee when no man pursueth. There is a kind of evil that loves darkness and secrecy. Satan has always worked through hidden systems, from the occult priests of Canaan to the secret combinations warned against in Scripture. The modern occult underground is simply the latest form of an ancient strategy.

This lesson will discuss Satanism, ritual abuse, and the occult underground. The subject is difficult. Some readers may have personal experience with it. Others may find the material disturbing. We will handle it with pastoral care. We will not sensationalize it. We will not use survivor stories to build a new theology. We will use them as illustrations of the darkness Christ has overcome and the healing He provides.

## 2. What Satanism Is and Is Not

Satanism is the deliberate worship or service of Satan. It exists in many forms, from the organized Church of Satan founded by Anton LaVey in 1966, to underground groups that practice criminal rituals, to lone individuals who have made personal pacts with demonic powers. Not every person who wears black clothing, listens to heavy metal music, or uses occult symbols is a Satanist. The church must not become a paranoid witch-hunt. At the same time, we must not deny that Satanism is real and growing.

LaVeyan Satanism is often atheistic. It does not believe in a literal Satan. It uses Satan as a symbol of rebellion, individualism, and self-worship. But even this atheistic form is spiritually dangerous because it exalts the self above God and removes every moral restraint. Theistic Satanism, by contrast, believes in and worships a real Satan or demonic beings. This form seeks power through rituals, blood, sexual acts, and blasphemy.

The Bible teaches that whatever is offered to false gods is offered to demons (1 Cor. 10:20). A person may call himself an atheistic Satanist and deny the existence of spirits, but the spiritual reality does not depend on his belief. When a person curses Christ, desecrates what is holy, and invites evil, the powers that answer are real. Satan does not care whether a person believes in him. He cares whether that person serves him.

## 3. Ritual Abuse: Listening Without Making It a New Orthodoxy

Ritual abuse is the abuse of a person, often a child, within an organized setting that uses occult or pseudo-religious ceremonies. Survivors describe ceremonies involving blood, sexual abuse, torture, animal sacrifice, and the deliberate desecration of Christian symbols. Some of these accounts are confirmed by law enforcement and therapeutic records. Others are complicated by false memories, media influence, and the suggestibility of trauma therapy.

The church must listen to survivors with compassion, but it must not treat every survivor testimony as inerrant theology. Survivors are witnesses to pain, not infallible interpreters of Satanic hierarchy. Their memories may be fragmented. Their perceptions may be shaped by fear and dissociation. Some details may be symbolic rather than literal. The compassionate response is to believe that suffering has occurred, to help the person find healing in Christ, and to test every theological claim against Scripture.

This is not a denial of ritual abuse. It is a protection against two errors. The first error is to dismiss all survivors as liars or lunatics. The second error is to build an entire doctrine of Satanic conspiracy from survivor narratives. The Bible is our final authority, not recovered memories, not popular books, and not sensational testimonies. Survivors need healing, not to be made into theologians.

## 4. The Lie Behind the Underground

The occult underground operates on a lie. It tells its victims that they are owned, that they cannot escape, that the cult has power everywhere, and that God has abandoned them. These are the same lies Satan tells every captive. He binds the mind before he binds the body. The truth of the gospel answers every one of these lies.

Colossians 1:13 says God has delivered us from the power of darkness and translated us into the kingdom of His dear Son. That transfer is real. It is legal. It is final. No cult, no ritual, no blood covenant made in childhood can stand against the blood of Jesus. Hebrews 9:12 says Christ entered into the holy place once for all, having obtained eternal redemption. His blood speaks a better word than the blood of every sacrifice ever offered to demons.

The occult underground also operates on secrecy. It threatens exposure, shame, and death to keep its victims silent. The church must be a safe place where secrets can come into the light. James 5:16 says, "Confess your faults one to another, and pray one for another, that ye may be healed." Healing happens when shame is replaced by grace and isolation is replaced by community.

## 5. How the Church Should Respond

The church's response to the occult underground must be balanced. We must not ignore it. We must not turn it into an obsession. We must not let it become the center of our message. The center of our message is Jesus Christ and Him crucified. Deliverance, healing, and justice flow from that center.

First, the church should teach clearly on the occult so that young people recognize the danger before they are captured by it. Second, the church should provide safe counseling and deliverance for those coming out of occult backgrounds. This requires trained, accountable, and compassionate ministers, not self-appointed experts who create dependency or exploit vulnerability. Third, the church should cooperate with lawful authorities when crimes such as abuse, trafficking, or murder are involved. Fourth, the church should pray against the hidden works of darkness and for the rescue of the oppressed.

Psalm 82:3–4 says, "Defend the poor and fatherless: do justice to the afflicted and needy. Deliver the poor and needy: rid them out of the hand of the wicked." This is the church's calling. We are not spectators. We are agents of rescue.

## 6. The Gospel for the Deepest Wounds
