**Renounce and reject.** In the name of Jesus, reject any word, image, or spirit attached to the dream. Say aloud, "I cancel every assignment of darkness from that dream. I reject it in Jesus' name. It has no authority over me." Speaking is important. Satan is not all-knowing; he hears your resistance.

**Take every thought captive.** Apply 2 Corinthians 10:5 immediately. Do not meditate on the dream. Do not analyze it endlessly. Do not tell everyone about it. Bring the thought to the obedience of Christ. Replace the image with Scripture.

**Pray and worship.** Psalm 149:6 says, "Let the high praises of God be in their mouth, and a twoedged sword in their hand." Praise and worship shift the atmosphere. Sing a hymn, quote a psalm, or declare God's goodness. This is especially effective after a night attack.

**Cleanse your environment.** If troubling dreams persist, examine your environment. What are you watching, reading, listening to, or allowing into your home? Are there objects, images, or relationships that have opened a door? Repent of any sin, remove any open door, and consecrate your home to the Lord. Some believers anoint their bedrooms with oil and pray Psalm 91 over their sleep.

**Seek counsel if needed.** If dreams are frequent, violent, or accompanied by physical manifestations, seek the help of mature believers. Do not seek out dream interpreters, psychics, or occult sources. Go to pastors, elders, or deliverance ministers who are grounded in Scripture.

## 6. The Believer's Protection in Sleep

God does not sleep. Psalm 121:3–4 says, "He will not suffer thy foot to be moved: he that keepeth thee will not slumber. Behold, he that keepeth Israel shall neither slumber nor sleep." The believer can rest because God guards the night. Psalm 4:8 says, "I will both lay me down in peace, and sleep: for thou, Lord, only makest me dwell in safety."

Before sleep, commit your mind to God. Psalm 139:23–24 is a good prayer: "Search me, O God, and know my heart: try me, and know my thoughts: and see if there be any wicked way in me, and lead me in the way everlasting." Ending the day with confession, forgiveness, and thanksgiving closes doors to the enemy.

You do not need to fear the night. You are not helpless in your sleep. The blood of Jesus covers you. The Holy Spirit dwells in you. Angels are ministering spirits sent to serve the heirs of salvation (Heb. 1:14). Sleep is a gift, not a battlefield you must dread.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Read Job 33:14–18, Acts 2:17, and 1 Thessalonians 5:21. Write a one-paragraph statement of your balanced view of dreams.
2. Examine the last three months. Have you had dreams that produced fear, confusion, or unusual spiritual pressure? Apply the four tests in section 3 to each one.
3. Before bed tonight, pray Psalm 91 aloud over yourself and your household. Do this for one week.
4. Remove any media, objects, or relationships that may be opening your spirit to deception or defilement in the night season.
5. If a troubling dream comes, refuse to discuss it with fear. Renounce it in Jesus' name, quote a Scripture, and worship until peace returns.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Dreams are a real but limited spiritual arena; Satan exploits the night season to terrify, defile, and deceive, so every dream must be tested by Scripture and brought under Christ’s authority.

**Key principle:** “Prove all things; hold fast that which is good” (1 Thessalonians 5:21).

**Core teaching points:**
- Dreams can come from God, the human soul, or demonic influence.
- Every dream must be tested by Scripture, by its fruit, and by whether it draws you to Christ.
- Satanic dream tactics include terror, sexual defilement, deceptive visitations, and false prophecies.
- The believer should renounce disturbing dreams, take thoughts captive, worship, and remove open doors.
- God guards sleep; the believer can rest in the promises of Psalm 91 and Psalm 4:8.

**Real-world example:** After watching occult-themed shows, a student has recurring nightmares and wakes fearful; she removes the media, prays Psalm 91 before bed, and renounces the dream each morning.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Either ignoring all dreams as meaningless or making major life decisions based on a vivid dream without Scripture and counsel.

**Practice assignment:** For one week, pray Psalm 91 aloud over yourself and your household before bed, and refuse to rehearse any frightening dream during the day.

**Worksheet idea:** “Night Season Journal”: record any significant dream, apply the four tests, write your response, and note any open door such as media, objects, or relationships.

**Completion requirement:** Pray Psalm 91 aloud each night for seven nights and write a one-paragraph balanced statement on how you will view dreams.

---

## Questions on Lesson 31 — Satan's Attack on Dreams, Visions, and the Night Season

- What are the three possible sources of a dream?
ANSWER: God, the human soul, and demonic influence.
- How should you respond to a disturbing dream?
ANSWER: Renounce it in Jesus’ name, take the thought captive, worship, remove any open door, and seek counsel if needed.



---

### Lesson 32 — Satan's Attack on Finances and Harvest

## 1. Money Is a Spiritual Matter

Money is never merely money in Scripture. It represents time, labor, trust, worship, and stewardship. Jesus spoke about money and possessions more than almost any other subject because He knew that the human heart attaches itself to treasure. Where your treasure is, there your heart will be also (Matt. 6:21). Satan understands this. He knows that if he can control your money, he can control your peace, your priorities, your generosity, and your obedience.

Satanic attack on finances takes many forms. Sometimes it is theft, loss, or unexpected disaster. Sometimes it is greed, fear, or laziness in the heart. Sometimes it is the "devourer" described in Malachi 3:11, a destructive force that consumes the fruit of labor. The believer must learn to recognize both external and internal attacks on finances and respond with faith, stewardship, and warfare.

## 2. The Devourer and the Withheld Tithe

Malachi 3:8–11 contains one of the most direct teachings on tithing and spiritual attack in the Bible. God accuses Israel of robbing Him in tithes and offerings. He invites them to test Him: "Bring ye all the tithes into the storehouse, that there may be meat in mine house, and prove me now herewith, saith the Lord of hosts, if I will not open you the windows of heaven, and pour you out a blessing, that there shall not be room enough to receive it. And I will rebuke the devourer for your sakes, and he shall not destroy the fruits of your ground; neither shall your vine cast her fruit before the time in the field, saith the Lord of hosts."

The devourer is a real spiritual force. The Hebrew idea is of something that eats up, wastes, or destroys. The devourer can work through sickness, accident, conflict, mismanagement, or any number of circumstances. The promise is that God will rebuke the devourer when His people honor Him with their firstfruits. This is not prosperity theology in the popular sense. It is a covenant principle: God blesses obedience, and He protects the harvest of those who put Him first.

Tithing is an act of worship, not a tax. It acknowledges that everything belongs to God and that He is the source of every increase. Abraham tithed to Melchizedek before the law (Gen. 14:20). Jacob vowed a tenth to God (Gen. 28:22). The tithe was incorporated into the law and affirmed in the prophets. In the New Testament, Jesus criticized the Pharisees for tithing mint and rue while neglecting justice and mercy, but He did not abolish tithing: "These ought ye to have done, and not to leave the other undone" (Luke 11:42).

## 3. Haggai and the Neglected House

Haggai 1:5–7 addresses another financial battlefield. The people had returned from exile and built their own paneled houses while the temple of the Lord lay in ruins. God says, "Consider your ways. Ye have sown much, and bring in little; ye eat, but ye have not enough; ye drink, but ye are not filled with drink; ye clothe you, but there is none warm; and he that earneth wages earneth wages to put it into a bag with holes."

This is a picture of futility. The people worked hard but saw no lasting fruit. Why? Because their priorities were wrong. They were building their own kingdom while neglecting God's. When the house of God is restored, the blessing returns. Haggai 1:8 says, "Go up to the mountain, and bring wood, and build the house; and I will take pleasure in it, and I will be glorified, saith the Lord."

The lesson for today is clear. A believer who neglects the kingdom of God while pursuing personal gain will often find his gain slipping through his fingers. The remedy is to put God's house first. This means faithful giving, service, worship, and obedience. It means asking, "Is my money flowing toward God's purposes, or only toward my own?"

## 4. Satan's Weapons: Theft, Greed, and Fear of Lack

Satan attacks finances through three primary weapons: theft, greed, and fear.

**Theft.** Satan is a thief. John 10:10 says, "The thief cometh not, but for to steal, and to kill, and to destroy." Theft can be literal — stolen property, fraud, lawsuits — or spiritual — stolen time, stolen opportunities, stolen seed. The believer must guard against theft by being wise, honest, and covered in prayer.

**Greed.** Greed is the internal thief. It is the love of money that destroys contentment and leads to sinful choices. First Timothy 6:10 warns, "For the love of money is the root of all evil: which while some coveted after, they have erred from the faith, and pierced themselves through with many sorrows." Greed makes a person vulnerable to scams, gambling, exploitation, and dishonest gain. The cure is generosity. Proverbs 11:24 says, "There is that scattereth, and yet increaseth; and there is that withholdeth more than is meet, but it tendeth to poverty."

**Fear of lack.** Fear says, "There will not be enough. I cannot give. I must hoard. I cannot trust God." This fear dishonors the God who supplies all our need according to His riches in glory (Phil. 4:19). It also paralyzes faith. Luke 6:38 says, "Give, and it shall be given unto you; good measure, pressed down, and shaken together, and running over, shall men give into your bosom." Giving is an expression of confidence in God's provision. Fear withholds; faith releases.

## 5. Sowing, Reaping, and Stewardship as Warfare

Second Corinthians 9:6–8 teaches the law of sowing and reaping in the kingdom: "He which soweth sparingly shall reap also sparingly; and he which soweth bountifully shall reap also bountifully. Every man according as he purposeth in his heart, so let him give; not grudgingly, or of necessity: for God loveth a cheerful giver. And God is able to make all grace abound toward you; that ye, always having all sufficiency in all things, may abound to every good work."

Sowing is not merely giving money. It is planting seed in faith. That seed can be money, time, mercy, kindness, or the Word. The principle is universal: what you sow, you reap (Gal. 6:7). Satan wants to keep you from sowing because he knows that a harvest threatens his kingdom. A generous church is a dangerous church. A generous believer is a hard target.

Stewardship is also warfare. How you handle what you have reveals whether you can be trusted with more. Luke 16:10 says, "He that is faithful in that which is least is faithful also in much." A believer who budgets wisely, avoids debt, pays debts, saves appropriately, and gives faithfully is fighting a financial battle with discipline. Slothfulness and disorder invite poverty and oppression.

## 6. Practical Steps to Financial Restoration

If your finances are under attack, here are practical steps to take.

**Repent of any dishonesty or greed.** Confess to God any theft, tax evasion, cheating, gambling, or covetousness. Proverbs 28:13 says, "He that covereth his sins shall not prosper: but whoso confesseth and forsaketh them shall have mercy."

**Restore the tithe.** If you have withheld the tithe, begin giving faithfully to your local church. This is an act of obedience and a rebuke to the devourer. Do not give to be blessed; give because God is worthy.

**Make a budget.** Know where your money goes. A budget is simply telling your money where to go instead of wondering where it went. Include giving, saving, debt reduction, and necessities.

**Break the power of debt.** Debt is a form of bondage. Proverbs 22:7 says, "The rich ruleth over the poor, and the borrower is servant to the lender." Make a plan to pay off debt aggressively. Avoid new debt except for wise, manageable investments.

**Give above the tithe.** Look for opportunities to sow into missions, the poor, and kingdom needs. Special offerings break the spirit of poverty and open new doors of blessing.

**Pray over your finances.** Anoint your wallet, your bank account, your business, and your job. Ask God to rebuke the devourer and to open the windows of heaven. Pray for wisdom and for favor.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Read Malachi 3:8–11 and Haggai 1:5–11 this week. Ask God to show you any place where you have withheld from Him.
2. Calculate your income and determine whether you are tithing faithfully. If not, begin this month.
3. Write out a simple budget. Identify one expense that can be reduced so that giving or debt repayment can increase.
4. Pray over your finances every day for thirty days. Ask God to rebuke the devourer and to guide your stewardship.
5. Look for one unexpected opportunity to give generously this week. Generosity breaks the grip of fear.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Money is a spiritual battlefield where Satan uses theft, greed, and fear to choke harvest. Faithful stewardship, tithing, and generosity break the devourer’s grip and open the windows of heaven.

**Key principle:** “Bring ye all the tithes into the storehouse, that there may be meat in mine house, and prove me now herewith, saith the Lord of hosts, if I will not open you the windows of heaven” (Malachi 3:10).

**Core teaching points:**
- Money reveals the heart; where treasure is, the heart follows.
- Malachi 3:8–11 shows that withholding the tithe invites the devourer, while faithful giving rebukes it.
- Haggai warns that building our own kingdom while neglecting God’s house leads to futility.
- Satan’s financial weapons are theft, greed, and fear of lack.
- Sowing, budgeting, debt reduction, and prayer are forms of financial warfare.

**Real-world example:** A family works hard but feels that money disappears; they discover they have not been tithing, begin giving faithfully, make a budget, and soon experience unexpected provision.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Treating tithing as a magic formula for wealth rather than an act of worship and covenant obedience.

**Practice assignment:** Calculate your income, begin faithful tithing this month, and create a simple budget that includes giving, saving, and debt reduction.

**Worksheet idea:** “Stewardship Snapshot”: income, current giving percentage, three expenses to reduce, debt list, and a thirty-day prayer plan for finances.

**Completion requirement:** Submit a written budget and evidence of tithing for one month.

---

## Questions on Lesson 32 — Satan's Attack on Finances and Harvest

- What is the devourer in Malachi 3:11?
ANSWER: A spiritual force that wastes or destroys the fruit of labor.
- What was the problem in Haggai 1?
ANSWER: The people built their own houses while neglecting God’s house.
- How does generosity fight fear?
ANSWER: Giving expresses confidence in God’s provision and breaks the spirit of poverty.



---

### Lesson 33 — Satan's Attack on Your Destiny and Calling

## 1. Every Life Has a God-Given Purpose

Before you ever took a breath, God knew you. Jeremiah 1:5 says, "Before I formed thee in the belly I knew thee; and before thou camest forth out of the womb I sanctified thee, and I ordained thee a prophet unto the nations." This is not only true of Jeremiah. It is true in principle of every person made in God's image. You were not an accident. You were not a random product of chance. You were known, sanctified, and ordained.

Ephesians 2:10 says, "For we are his workmanship, created in Christ Jesus unto good works, which God hath before ordained that we should walk in them." God prepared good works for you to walk in. Romans 8:28–30 describes the golden chain of destiny: those who love God are called according to His purpose, foreknown, predestined, called, justified, and glorified. Your calling is not a vague possibility. It is a sovereign intention.

Because destiny matters, Satan attacks it. He cannot ultimately prevent God's purpose, but he can delay it, distort it, discourage the pursuit of it, and keep the believer from fully entering it. The story of Scripture is full of men and women whose callings were attacked: Joseph sold into slavery, Moses exiled to Midian, David hunted by Saul, Esther threatened by Haman, Nehemiah opposed by Sanballat, Jesus tempted in the wilderness, Paul stoned and imprisoned. In every case, the attack was against the calling.

## 2. How Satan Steals Vision

A calling begins with vision: a sense of what God wants to do through you. Satan's first move is often to steal, blur, or replace that vision. He does this in several ways.

He plants **doubt about the call itself**. "Who are you to do that? You are not qualified. You must have imagined it." This is the same attack he used on Moses, who protested that he was slow of speech (Ex. 4:10). It is the attack he used on Gideon, who said his family was the least and he was the least in his family (Judg. 6:15). God does not call the qualified; He qualifies the called.

He introduces **discouragement through delay**. When the promise does not arrive on our timetable, we conclude that God has changed His mind. Abraham waited twenty-five years for Isaac. Joseph waited thirteen years in slavery and prison. David waited years in caves before taking the throne. Delay is not denial. Satan wants you to quit in the waiting room.

He uses **distraction**. The enemy does not need to destroy you if he can simply divert you. Good things can become substitutes for God-things. Busyness, hobbies, relationships, even ministry activities can pull you away from your primary assignment. Luke 10:42 records Jesus saying to Martha, "But one thing is needful: and Mary hath chosen that good part, which shall not be taken away from her." The enemy of your destiny is not always sin; sometimes it is the second-best.

## 3. The Spirit of Delay and Distraction

Satan often works through what the Bible calls hindrances. First Thessalonians 2:18 says, "Wherefore we would have come unto you, even I Paul, once and again; but Satan hindered us." Daniel 10:12–13 describes a spiritual conflict that delayed the answer to Daniel's prayer for twenty-one days. The prince of Persia withstood the angel until Michael came to help. These passages reveal that spiritual opposition can produce real delays in the fulfillment of God's will.

The spirit of delay works by wearing down the believer. It creates a long series of obstacles: closed doors, broken relationships, financial pressure, health issues, and misunderstandings. The believer begins to feel that nothing ever works out. The calling starts to seem impossible. This is the intended effect. Satan knows that a discouraged believer is easier to defeat than an angry one.

The spirit of distraction works differently. It fills life with noise so that the calling cannot be heard. It makes the urgent crowd out the important. It convinces the believer that tomorrow will be a better time to obey. Proverbs 6:4 says, "Give not sleep to thine eyes, nor slumber to thine eyelids" when you have made a vow. The time to obey is now.

## 4. Reclaiming Your Calling

Reclaiming a calling requires clarity, courage, and persistence. The first step is to return to the original word God spoke. Habakkuk 2:2 says, "Write the vision, and make it plain upon tables, that he may run that readeth it." If God has spoken a purpose to you, write it down. Keep it before your eyes. When the fog rolls in, the written vision is your anchor.

The second step is to repent of compromise and distraction. Acts 3:19 says, "Repent ye therefore, and be converted, that your sins may be blotted out, when the times of refreshing shall come from the presence of the Lord." Repentance is not only for gross sin. It is for turning back to God when you have turned aside.

The third step is to take the next small step of obedience. Destiny is not fulfilled in one dramatic leap. It is fulfilled in a thousand small yeses. Moses' first step was to pick up his staff. David's first step was to visit his brothers. Nehemiah's first step was to inspect the walls at night. Your next step may be small, but it is decisive.

The fourth step is to resist the spirit of delay. Speak to the delays in Jesus' name. Claim the promises God has given you. Pray like Daniel, who refused to give up until the answer came. Surround yourself with people who believe in your calling and will pray you through the fog.

## 5. The Holy Spirit and the Rekindled Gift

Second Timothy 1:6–7 is a crucial passage for anyone whose calling feels cold: "Wherefore I put thee in remembrance that thou stir up the gift of God, which is in thee by the putting on of my hands. For God hath not given us the spirit of fear; but of power, and of love, and of a sound mind."

Paul tells Timothy to "stir up" the gift. The word means to rekindle, as one would blow on embers to restart a fire. Gifts and callings can grow cold through neglect, discouragement, or sin. They are not lost; they are dormant. The Holy Spirit can rekindle them. The believer must cooperate by fanning the flame through prayer, worship, practice, and renewed commitment.

Fear is the great enemy of calling. God has not given us a spirit of fear. The fear of failure, rejection, inadequacy, or man will paralyze destiny. The Spirit gives power, love, and a sound mind. Power to do what God has called you to do. Love to keep serving even when it is hard. A sound mind to think clearly and make wise decisions.

## 6. Your Calling Is Bigger Than Your Comfort

Many believers never fulfill their calling because they will not leave their comfort zone. But God does not call us to comfort; He calls us to Christlikeness and mission. The calling will cost something. It will require faith, sacrifice, and perseverance. It will require saying no to lesser things so that you can say yes to greater ones.

Your calling is also not only about you. It is about the people God wants to touch through you. When Satan delays you, he delays the blessing of many. When you step into your calling, you release grace to others. Romans 8:30 says those whom God glorifies are those He has called. The end of your calling is glory — the display of God's goodness in your life and through your life.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Spend time in prayer asking God to restore the original vision He gave you. Write it down in one clear sentence.
2. Identify one area where you have allowed delay, distraction, or discouragement to stall your calling.
3. Memorize 2 Timothy 1:7. When fear rises, quote it aloud.
4. Take one concrete step this week toward your calling, even if it is small. Obedience activates destiny.
5. Ask two or three trusted believers to pray for you regularly in this season. Calling is not fulfilled in isolation.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Every believer has a God-ordained purpose, so Satan attacks destiny through doubt, delay, and distraction. Reclaiming your calling requires clarity, repentance, and faithful next steps.

**Key principle:** “For we are his workmanship, created in Christ Jesus unto good works, which God hath before ordained that we should walk in them” (Ephesians 2:10).

**Core teaching points:**
- God foreknows, calls, and prepares good works for each believer.
- Satan steals vision through doubt, discouragement from delay, and distraction with lesser things.
- Delay is not denial; the spirit of delay can be resisted by persistent faith.
- Calling is reclaimed by writing the vision, repenting of compromise, and taking the next small step.
- The Holy Spirit gives power, love, and a sound mind to overcome the fear that paralyzes destiny.

**Real-world example:** A young woman feels called to mentor teens but accepts a second job that exhausts her; after prayer, she declines the extra job, writes her vision, and volunteers one evening a week.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Waiting for a dramatic confirmation before taking the next small step of obedience.

**Practice assignment:** Write your calling in one clear sentence, identify one distraction or delay, and take one concrete step this week toward it.

**Worksheet idea:** “Destiny Map”: original vision, current obstacle, small next step, two people who will pray for you, and a daily 2 Timothy 1:7 confession.

**Completion requirement:** Share your one-sentence vision with a trusted believer and report completing one concrete step toward it.

---

## Questions on Lesson 33 — Satan's Attack on Your Destiny and Calling

- How does Satan attack vision?
ANSWER: Through doubt, discouragement from delay, and distraction.
- What does 2 Timothy 1:6 say to do with a cold gift?
ANSWER: Stir it up.
- Why is calling bigger than comfort?
ANSWER: God calls us to Christlikeness and mission, not to ease.



---

### Lesson 34 — Satan's Attack on Joy, Peace, and Emotional Health

## 1. The Strategic Value of Joy and Peace

Joy and peace are not luxuries in the Christian life. They are strategic weapons. Nehemiah 8:10 declares, "The joy of the Lord is your strength." When joy is gone, strength is gone. Philippians 4:6–7 promises that prayer with thanksgiving produces the peace of God, which guards heart and mind. Isaiah 26:3 says, "Thou wilt keep him in perfect peace, whose mind is stayed on thee: because he trusteth in thee." John 14:27 records Jesus saying, "Peace I leave with you, my peace I give unto you: not as the world giveth, give I unto you. Let not your heart be troubled, neither let it be afraid."

Satan knows that a joyless, anxious believer is a weak believer. He knows that peace is the platform from which faith operates. That is why he attacks joy and peace with persistent precision. His goal is not to make you sin in some dramatic way. His goal is to make you weary, troubled, and afraid. A person who loses joy will lose endurance. A person who loses peace will lose perspective.

## 2. How Satan Steals Joy

Satan steals joy through several common strategies.

**Circumstances.** He points to every problem — health, money, relationships, disappointments — and says, "How can you rejoice in this?" Circumstantial joy is fragile, but biblical joy is rooted in something unshakable. Habakkuk 3:17–18 says, "Although the fig tree shall not blossom, neither shall fruit be in the vines; the labour of the olive shall fail, and the fields shall yield no meat; the flock shall be cut off from the fold, and there shall be no herd in the stalls: yet I will rejoice in the Lord, I will joy in the God of my salvation." Joy is a choice to look at God when everything else is barren.

**Offense.** Satan keeps old wounds fresh. He replays conversations, magnifies insults, and nurtures grudges. Offense robs joy because it fixes the mind on what someone did rather than on what God is doing. Hebrews 12:15 warns, "Looking diligently lest any man fail of the grace of God; lest any root of bitterness springing up trouble you, and thereby many be defiled." Forgiveness is joy's guardian.

**Discouragement.** Discouragement looks at the gap between the promise and the present reality. It says, "Nothing is changing. God has forgotten." Satan used discouragement against the Israelites in the wilderness and against the exiles rebuilding the temple. The antidote is to remember what God has already done and to praise Him before the answer arrives.

**Condemnation.** When a believer sins or is accused, Satan works to remove all sense of joy by saying, "You are too damaged to rejoice." But Romans 8:1 says, "There is therefore now no condemnation to them which are in Christ Jesus." Joy is restored by receiving forgiveness and returning to praise.

## 3. How Satan Steals Peace

Peace is stolen through anxiety, fear, and disorder.

**Anxiety.** Anxiety rehearses the future with dread. It imagines problems that have not happened and tries to solve them all at once. Philippians 4:6–7 says, "Be careful for nothing; but in every thing by prayer and supplication with thanksgiving let your requests be made known unto God. And the peace of God, which passeth all understanding, shall keep your hearts and minds through Christ Jesus." Prayer is the exchange of anxiety for peace.

**Fear.** Fear focuses on the threat rather than the protector. It forgets that God is a refuge and strength, a very present help in trouble (Ps. 46:1). First John 4:18 says perfect love casts out fear. The more the believer rests in the Father's love, the less room fear has.

**Disorder.** A life without order is a life without peace. Chaos in schedule, relationships, finances, or home environment creates a constant low-level stress that wears down the soul. God is a God of order (1 Cor. 14:33). Sometimes the most spiritual thing you can do is organize your life, set boundaries, and say no.

## 4. Depression, Anxiety, Despair, and the Believer's Response

The Bible does not ignore emotional suffering. The Psalms are full of raw cries: "Why art thou cast down, O my soul?" (Ps. 42:5). Elijah sat under a juniper tree and asked God to take his life (1 Kings 19:4). Job cursed the day of his birth (Job 3:1). Jesus Himself was a man of sorrows and acquainted with grief (Isa. 53:3). Emotional pain is part of the human condition in a fallen world.

However, the believer has resources that the world does not have. The Holy Spirit produces joy and peace as fruit (Gal. 5:22). The Word of God is a source of hope (Rom. 15:4). Prayer brings relief (Phil. 4:6–7). Community provides strength (Eccles. 4:9–10). Worship lifts the spirit (Ps. 42:5). Confession and forgiveness remove guilt (1 John 1:9).

Yet the believer's response must also be honest. Spiritual warfare is real, but not every emotional struggle is purely demonic. Sometimes the body is sick. Sometimes the mind is exhausted. Sometimes trauma has not healed. Sometimes medication and therapy are necessary tools of God's common grace. The church has sometimes erred by treating every depression as a demon to cast out. That approach can add shame to suffering.

## 5. When Spiritual Warfare Needs Professional Help

There is a place for professional help in the believer's life. Doctors, counselors, psychiatrists, and therapists can be gifts of God. Luke, the author of one of the Gospels and Acts, was a physician (Col. 4:14). Jesus healed bodies supernaturally, but He also affirmed that the sick need a physician (Matt. 9:12). The same principle applies to mental and emotional health.

If you are experiencing persistent depression, panic attacks, suicidal thoughts, hallucinations, severe mood swings, or an inability to function in daily life, you should seek professional help. This is not a failure of faith. It is wisdom. A soldier with a broken leg needs a medic and a prayer. The two are not enemies.

At the same time, professional help should not replace spiritual weapons. The counselor can help the mind; the Spirit renews the soul. Medication can stabilize chemistry; the Word stabilizes identity. The best care is integrated: prayer, community, the Word, and competent professionals working together.

If emotional suffering is connected to trauma, spiritual oppression, or generational patterns, a pastor or deliverance minister may also be needed. But the goal is always healing and wholeness, not merely symptom management.

## 6. Rejoicing in the Lord Always

Philippians 4:4 says, "Rejoice in the Lord alway: and again I say, Rejoice." This is not a command to feel happy every moment. It is a command to return our joy to its source. We rejoice in the Lord. He does not change. His promises do not fail. His love does not waver. His presence does not leave. When everything else is uncertain, He is the fixed point.

Peace and joy are restored by returning to the basics: gratitude, worship, Scripture, prayer, fellowship, and obedience. These practices may not remove the problem immediately, but they reorient the soul. They remind us that the battle is not ours alone. The Lord is our shepherd; we shall not want. He makes us lie down in green pastures; He leads us beside still waters; He restores our soul (Ps. 23:1–3).

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Identify the primary way Satan is currently stealing your joy or peace: circumstance, offense, discouragement, condemnation, anxiety, fear, or disorder.
2. Write out one Scripture that directly answers that attack. Read it aloud every morning and every evening for seven days.
3. Schedule a time of uninterrupted worship this week. Do not wait until you feel like it. Worship is an act of war.
4. If you are struggling with persistent emotional pain, tell a trusted believer and consider seeking professional help. Do not suffer alone.
5. Practice Philippians 4:6–7 by turning every anxious thought into a specific prayer. Keep a worry list and cross each item off as you pray it.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Joy and peace are strategic weapons in the Christian life; Satan seeks to make believers weary, anxious, and offended, but these can be restored through worship, prayer, Scripture, and appropriate professional care.

**Key principle:** “Be careful for nothing; but in every thing by prayer and supplication with thanksgiving let your requests be made known unto God. And the peace of God, which passeth all understanding, shall keep your hearts and minds through Christ Jesus” (Philippians 4:6–7).

**Core teaching points:**
- The joy of the Lord is strength, and the peace of God guards heart and mind.
- Satan steals joy through circumstances, offense, discouragement, and condemnation.
- Satan steals peace through anxiety, fear, and disorder.
- Emotional suffering is real; spiritual weapons and professional help are not enemies.
- Rejoicing in the Lord reorients the soul regardless of circumstances.

**Real-world example:** A man loses a job and spirals into anxiety, snapping at his wife; he begins a daily worry list, turns each item into prayer, and schedules a worship time, noticing peace return.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Believing that persistent anxiety or depression is solely a spiritual failure and refusing medical or counseling help.

**Practice assignment:** For seven days, keep a worry list, turn each item into a specific prayer with thanksgiving, and cross it off once you have prayed.

**Worksheet idea:** “Peace Inventory”: identify which joy or peace thief is active, list three truths about God’s character, and plan one worship session and one conversation with a trusted believer.

**Completion requirement:** Complete a seven-day worry-to-prayer list and document one worship session.

---

## Questions on Lesson 34 — Satan's Attack on Joy, Peace, and Emotional Health

- What does Nehemiah 8:10 say is your strength?
ANSWER: The joy of the Lord.
- How is peace stolen?
ANSWER: Through anxiety, fear, and disorder.
- When should a believer seek professional help for emotional pain?
ANSWER: When symptoms persist, impair daily life, or include suicidal thoughts.



---

### Lesson 35 — Satan's Attack on Health and the Body

## 1. The Body Matters to God

The human body is not a temporary shell to be escaped. It is a temple of the Holy Spirit (1 Cor. 6:19), a member of Christ (1 Cor. 6:15), and an instrument for righteousness (Rom. 6:13). God created the body good. He will resurrect the body glorified. He cares about the body in this life and the next. Therefore, Satan attacks the body. Sickness, weakness, pain, and disability are tools he uses to discourage, disable, and destroy.

The Bible distinguishes between ordinary illness and Satanic affliction, but the line is not always easy to draw. Job's boils came through Satan's direct action (Job 2:7). The woman bound by a spirit of infirmity for eighteen years was healed by Jesus, who said, "Whom Satan hath bound" (Luke 13:16). The epileptic boy's condition involved a demonic element (Matt. 17:15–18). On the other hand, Timothy had frequent illnesses that Paul treated with practical advice about wine (1 Tim. 5:23). Trophimus was left sick at Miletus (2 Tim. 4:20). Paul had a thorn in the flesh that God chose not to remove (2 Cor. 12:7–10).

This means the believer must avoid two extremes. One extreme says every sickness is from the devil and can be instantly healed by faith. The other extreme says sickness is only physical and prayer is irrelevant. The biblical position is more nuanced: the body is subject to the fallen world, Satan can attack health, God can heal supernaturally, and God also uses medicine, rest, and wisdom.

## 2. Healing in the Atonement

Exodus 15:26 calls Jehovah-Rapha, the Lord who heals you. Psalm 103:2–3 says, "Bless the Lord, O my soul, and forget not all his benefits: who forgiveth all thine iniquities; who healeth all thy diseases." Isaiah 53:5 prophesies of the Messiah, "But he was wounded for our transgressions, he was bruised for our iniquities: the chastisement of our peace was upon him; and with his stripes we are healed." First Peter 2:24 applies this to believers: "Who his own self bare our sins in his own body on the tree, that we, being dead to sins, should live unto righteousness: by whose stripes ye were healed."

These texts teach that healing is connected to the atonement. Just as Jesus bore our sins, He bore our sicknesses. This is why Matthew 8:17 says Jesus healed the sick "that it might be fulfilled which was spoken by Esaias the prophet, saying, Himself took our infirmities, and bare our sicknesses." Healing is part of the package of redemption. The believer has a right to expect health as part of the blessings purchased at the cross.

At the same time, healing is not always immediate or automatic in this life. The atonement guarantees ultimate healing — the resurrection body will never get sick — but in the present age, the kingdom is already and not yet. We see in part. We pray for healing, believe for healing, and often receive healing, but we also live in bodies that are still subject to death. Faith does not deny this tension; it presses through it.

## 3. Satanic Sickness vs. Ordinary Illness

How can a believer discern whether a sickness has a Satanic element? Here are some indicators.

**Suddenness and severity.** Illnesses that come out of nowhere with unusual timing — right before a mission, ministry opportunity, or spiritual breakthrough — may be more than coincidence. Satan times his attacks.

**Resistance to natural treatment.** When medical care produces no improvement and the condition defies diagnosis, a spiritual element may be involved.

**Association with spiritual activity.** If the sickness began after occult involvement, a curse, a traumatic sin, or a spiritual attack in another area, it may be connected.

**Atmosphere of oppression.** Sickness accompanied by fear, despair, nightmares, or a sense of spiritual heaviness may indicate more than a physical cause.

**Words of knowledge or prophecy.** Sometimes the Holy Spirit reveals that an illness is demonic or rooted in a spiritual issue. This must be confirmed carefully and humbly.

None of these indicators alone proves Satanic causation. They are clues to be prayed over. The first response to any sickness should be prayer, regardless of the cause.

## 4. The Prayer of Faith and the Laying On of Hands

James 5:14–15 gives the church's protocol for sickness: "Is any sick among you? let him call for the elders of the church; and let them pray over him, anointing him with oil in the name of the Lord: and the prayer of faith shall save the sick, and the Lord shall raise him up; and if he have committed sins, they shall be forgiven him."

The prayer of faith is not a formula. It is a prayer offered in confidence that God hears and heals. The oil symbolizes the Holy Spirit and consecration. The elders represent the authority and care of the church. The sick person is not passive; he calls for help. Healing often comes in community, not isolation.

The prayer of faith does not guarantee healing on demand. God is sovereign. But it does release faith, invite the Spirit's power, and align the sick person with God's will. Sometimes healing is immediate. Sometimes it is gradual. Sometimes it does not come in this life. Faith means trusting God in all three outcomes.

## 5. Faith, Prayer, Medical Care, and Confession

The believer should use every legitimate means of healing. Faith and medicine are not enemies. They are allies.

**Faith.** Believe what God has said about healing. Speak His promises. Refuse to let symptoms have the final word. Jesus asked the blind men, "Believe ye that I am able to do this?" (Matt. 9:28). Faith opens the door.

**Prayer.** Pray personally. Ask others to pray. Fast if led. Persist like the importunate widow (Luke 18:1–8). Do not give up because the answer is delayed.

**Medical care.** Go to doctors, take medicine, follow treatment, and use wisdom. Paul told Timothy to use wine for his stomach's sake (1 Tim. 5:23). Luke was a beloved physician. Medical knowledge is a gift from God.

**Confession.** If sin has opened the door to sickness, confess it. James 5:16 says, "Confess your faults one to another, and pray one for another, that ye may be healed." Some sicknesses are connected to bitterness, unforgiveness, immorality, or occult involvement. Confession and repentance close those doors.

## 6. The Long Road of Faithful Suffering

Some believers do not receive the healing they seek in this life. This is not a condemnation of their faith. It is part of the mystery of suffering in a fallen world. Paul prayed three times for his thorn to be removed, and God said no (2 Cor. 12:8–9). His grace was sufficient. His strength was made perfect in weakness.

For those walking through long illness, the battle is not only for healing but for faith, joy, peace, and purpose. Satan wants the sick person to become bitter, isolated, and useless. God wants the sick person to become a testimony of grace. Many of the greatest saints in history ministered powerfully from sickbeds. Their bodies were weak, but their spirits were mighty.

The body is the Lord's. Whether healed or waiting for resurrection healing, the believer can glorify God in the body. The goal is not merely to feel better. It is to honor Christ in every condition.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Read Exodus 15:26, Psalm 103:2–3, Isaiah 53:5, and 1 Peter 2:24. Meditate on what Jesus purchased for you at the cross.
2. If you are sick, ask God to reveal whether there is a spiritual root: unconfessed sin, unforgiveness, occult involvement, or a specific Satanic attack.
3. Pray for healing, and also take practical steps: see a doctor, rest, eat wisely, and follow medical advice. Faith and wisdom work together.
4. If you know someone who is sick, offer to pray with them and, if appropriate, ask the elders of your church to anoint them with oil.
5. If you are walking through a long illness, declare daily: "The Lord is my shepherd; I shall not want. His grace is sufficient for me."

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** The body is the temple of the Holy Spirit and a target of Satan. Believers should pray for healing, use medical wisdom, confess sin, and honor Christ in sickness or in health.

**Key principle:** “Who his own self bare our sins in his own body on the tree, that we, being dead to sins, should live unto righteousness: by whose stripes ye were healed” (1 Peter 2:24).

**Core teaching points:**
- The body is the temple of the Holy Spirit and an instrument for righteousness.
- Healing is connected to the atonement; Jesus bore our sicknesses.
- Some sickness may have a Satanic element, but not all; discernment is needed.
- James 5:14–15 gives the church protocol: prayer of faith and anointing with oil.
- Faith, medical care, and confession work together; long illness can still glorify God.

**Real-world example:** A woman with chronic migraines prays for healing, sees a neurologist, forgives a relative, and asks elders to anoint her; over time the attacks lessen and she becomes a source of encouragement.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Insisting every sickness is purely demonic and rejecting medicine, or assuming sickness is purely physical and never praying.

**Practice assignment:** If you are sick, pray for healing, take one practical medical step, and ask one mature believer to pray with you this week.

**Worksheet idea:** “Body and Soul Check”: describe the condition, list any spiritual indicators, note medical steps taken, identify any unforgiveness or sin, and write a daily healing confession from Scripture.

**Completion requirement:** Document prayer for healing, any medical step taken, and one Scripture declaration repeated daily.

---

## Questions on Lesson 35 — Satan's Attack on Health and the Body

- What does 1 Corinthians 6:19 call the body?
ANSWER: The temple of the Holy Spirit.
- What is the prayer of faith in James 5:14–15?
ANSWER: The elders pray over the sick, anoint with oil, and the Lord raises him up.
- Why did Paul not receive healing for his thorn?
ANSWER: God’s grace was sufficient, and His strength was made perfect in weakness.



---

### Lesson 36 — Generational Curses, Foundations, and Satanic Exchange

## 1. The Reality of Generational Patterns

The Bible teaches that sin has consequences beyond the individual. Exodus 20:5 says God visits "the iniquity of the fathers upon the children unto the third and fourth generation of them that hate me." Numbers 14:18 repeats the same truth. This is not arbitrary punishment. It is the natural and spiritual result of patterns that are passed down through family lines: addiction, abuse, idolatry, sexual immorality, violence, divorce, poverty, occult involvement, and rebellion.

When a father worships an idol, his children grow up in an atmosphere where that idol seems normal. When a mother speaks bitterness, her children learn to speak bitterness. When grandparents open doors to the occult, later generations may experience fear, oppression, and spiritual disturbance without knowing why. Sin reproduces itself. Iniquity is not merely an individual act; it is a bent, a twisted pattern that can be inherited.

However, the Bible also limits this truth. Ezekiel 18:1–20 strongly rejects the idea that children are morally guilty for their fathers' sins. "The soul that sinneth, it shall die. The son shall not bear the iniquity of the father" (Ezek. 18:20). This is the other side of the coin. We are not condemned for what our ancestors did, but we can be affected by the environment, patterns, and spiritual consequences they left behind.

## 2. Iniquity, Sin, and Transgression

To understand generational impact, it helps to distinguish three biblical words. **Sin** is the act of missing God's mark. **Transgression** is the deliberate crossing of a known boundary. **Iniquity** is the deeper bent or crookedness that produces repeated sin. Iniquity is the root; sin is the fruit.

Iniquity can be passed down. Psalm 51:5 says, "Behold, I was shapen in iniquity; and in sin did my mother conceive me." This does not mean David's mother sinned in conceiving him. It means he was born into a fallen human condition that carried the bent toward sin. That bent is part of every family line until the cross interrupts it.

Satan exploits iniquity. Where there is a family pattern of lust, he returns to it. Where there is a family pattern of fear, he reinforces it. Where there is a family pattern of poverty or failure, he whispers, "This is just how it is for us." He uses generational patterns to make sin feel inevitable.

## 3. The Believer's Freedom in Christ

Galatians 3:13 is the great liberation text: "Christ hath redeemed us from the curse of the law, being made a curse for us: for it is written, Cursed is every one that hangeth on a tree." The believer in Christ is no longer under the curse of the law, including the curse of generational iniquity. The legal ground for the curse has been removed by the blood of Jesus.

Romans 8:1–2 says, "There is therefore now no condemnation to them which are in Christ Jesus, who walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit. For the law of the Spirit of life in Christ Jesus hath made me free from the law of sin and death." The law of sin and death includes generational bondage. The law of the Spirit of life sets the believer free.

This freedom is not merely theoretical. It is practical. The believer can look at a family pattern and say, "That ends with me." Addiction, abuse, occult bondage, fear, poverty, and rebellion do not have to continue. The cross breaks the chain. The Holy Spirit can rewire the family trajectory.

## 4. Satanic Exchange and the Believer's Response

Sometimes generational bondage is strengthened by what the Bible calls a Satanic exchange. This happens when a person or family trades something God-given for something Satan offers. Esau traded his birthright for a bowl of stew (Gen. 25:29–34). Achan brought a curse on his family by stealing devoted things (Josh. 7). Idolatry is an exchange: the worship due to God is given to demons (1 Cor. 10:20). Sexual immorality is an exchange: the body belongs to Christ but is joined to another (1 Cor. 6:15–20). Occult involvement is an exchange: guidance from God is replaced by guidance from forbidden spirits.

Satanic exchange can happen knowingly or unknowingly. A family may dedicate a child to an idol. A person may make a vow to a false god. A business may be founded on occult practices. A home may be built on stolen land. These exchanges create spiritual claims that affect later generations. The believer must identify them, repent of them, and renounce them in Jesus' name.

## 5. Closing Generational Doors

Closing generational doors involves several steps.

**Identify the pattern.** Ask the Holy Spirit to reveal recurring sins, failures, sicknesses, fears, or oppressions in your family line. Look at three or four generations if you can. Common patterns include premature death, divorce, poverty, mental illness, addiction, violence, sexual brokenness, and occult involvement.

**Confess and renounce.** Confess the sins of your ancestors as though they were your own family sins. This does not mean you are personally guilty of them. It means you are acknowledging the iniquity and taking responsibility to break it. Say something like, "I confess the sin of idolatry, adultery, witchcraft, bitterness, or poverty in my family line. I renounce it. I reject its claim on my life and on my children."

**Apply the blood of Jesus.** Declare that Christ has redeemed you from the curse of the law. Plead the blood of Jesus over the family line. The blood is the legal basis for freedom. Revelation 12:11 says the saints overcame the accuser by the blood of the Lamb and the word of their testimony.

**Replace with blessing.** Do not only close the door to curse; open the door to blessing. Proclaim the promises of God over your family. Ask God to turn the iniquity into a testimony. Joel 2:25 says God restores the years that the locust has eaten.

**Walk in obedience.** Freedom must be maintained. Do not return to the sins that opened the door. Keep the family altar. Teach your children the ways of God. Remove occult objects and practices. Live transparently before the Lord.

## 6. The Balance of Responsibility and Grace

Generational curse teaching can become harmful when it produces fear, condemnation, or a constant search for hidden demons in every problem. The believer should not become obsessed with the family line. The focus must always be on Christ. The cross is greater than the curse. The blood is stronger than the iniquity.

At the same time, the believer should not ignore real patterns. Pretending generational impact does not exist leaves doors open that Jesus wants closed. Wisdom discerns the difference between ordinary life struggles and recurring spiritual bondage.

If you are uncertain whether a problem is generational, seek godly counsel. A mature pastor or deliverance minister can help you discern, pray, and close doors. The goal is freedom, not fear.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Draw a simple family tree or list three to four generations. Ask God to reveal any recurring patterns of sin, sickness, failure, or oppression.
2. Read Ezekiel 18:1–20 and Galatians 3:13. Write a declaration that you are free in Christ from every generational curse.
3. In prayer, confess and renounce any iniquity in your family line. Apply the blood of Jesus by faith.
4. Remove any objects, practices, or relationships in your home that may represent occult involvement or open doors from the past.
5. Establish a new family pattern of prayer, worship, and obedience. Ask God to make your family a generation that follows Him fully.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** Sin can leave patterns across generations, but the cross breaks every curse. Believers must identify family iniquity, renounce it, apply the blood of Jesus, and establish new patterns of obedience.

**Key principle:** “Christ hath redeemed us from the curse of the law, being made a curse for us: for it is written, Cursed is every one that hangeth on a tree” (Galatians 3:13).

**Core teaching points:**
- Exodus 20:5 reveals that iniquity affects children; Ezekiel 18 limits it by individual responsibility.
- Iniquity is the bent that produces sin; Satan exploits family patterns.
- Galatians 3:13 says Christ redeemed us from the curse of the law.
- Satanic exchange trades God-given blessings for what Satan offers and must be identified and renounced.
- Generational doors are closed through confession, the blood, blessing, and ongoing obedience.

**Real-world example:** A man notices alcoholism, divorce, and fear across three generations; he confesses and renounces the pattern, removes occult items from his childhood home, and starts family devotions.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Becoming obsessed with hidden generational demons, or dismissing all family patterns as coincidence.

**Practice assignment:** Draw a simple family tree of three to four generations, note recurring patterns, write a freedom declaration, and renounce iniquity in prayer.

**Worksheet idea:** “Family Line Inventory”: list patterns across generations, identify possible Satanic exchanges, write the renunciation, and list new practices to establish in your home.

**Completion requirement:** Submit a family pattern inventory and a written declaration of freedom in Christ.

## Questions on Lesson 36 — Generational Curses, Foundations, and Satanic Exchange

- What is the difference between sin and iniquity?
ANSWER: Sin is the act; iniquity is the bent or crookedness that produces repeated sin.
- What does Galatians 3:13 say Christ did?
ANSWER: Redeemed us from the curse of the law.



---

## Part VI — Spiritual Warfare, Deliverance, and Closing Doors

### Lesson 37 — The Whole Armor of God

## 1. Why the Armor Matters

Every soldier who walks onto a battlefield understands one thing: the fight is real, and protection is not optional. Paul ends the letter to the Ephesians with exactly this kind of urgency. He does not close with pleasantries. He closes with a call to arms. "Finally, my brethren, be strong in the Lord, and in the power of his might. Put on the whole armour of God, that ye may be able to stand against the wiles of the devil" (Eph. 6:10–11).

The phrase "whole armour" is important. Paul does not suggest a piece or two. He insists on the complete outfit. A soldier who wears a breastplate but no helmet, or carries a sword but no shield, is a soldier who has left part of his body exposed. In the same way, the believer who tries to fight Satan with faith alone, or with Scripture alone, or with prayer alone, leaves other areas open to attack. Every piece has a purpose. Every piece protects a part of the believer's life that Satan targets.

The armor is not a metaphor for self-improvement. It is not Christian self-help dressed in military language. It is the provision of God for the conflict of the Christian life. The warfare is spiritual. The enemy is not flesh and blood. The weapons and protection must therefore come from God Himself. This lesson walks through each piece, explains what it protects, and shows how to put it on as a daily discipline.

## 2. Be Strong in the Lord: The Foundation of All Armor

Before Paul lists the armor, he gives the precondition. "Be strong in the Lord, and in the power of his might" (Eph. 6:10). This is not a call to self-generated strength. It is a call to draw strength from the Lord. The believer does not become strong by trying harder. The believer becomes strong by abiding in Christ, remembering who He is, and relying on what He has already accomplished.

This matters because many Christians approach spiritual warfare backwards. They see the enemy, feel afraid, and then try to manufacture courage. The biblical order is the opposite. First, we remember the Lord. We remember that He is greater than the one who is in the world (1 John 4:4). We remember that we are in Christ, seated in heavenly places (Eph. 2:6). Then, from that place of security, we put on the armor and stand.

The armor is for standing, not for running. Three times in Ephesians 6 Paul says the goal is to stand: "that ye may be able to stand" (v. 11), "that ye may be able to withstand in the evil day" (v. 13), "stand therefore" (v. 14). The Christian does not charge into the darkness to prove his bravery. He stands his ground because Christ has already won the territory. The armor keeps him upright when the pressure comes.

## 3. The Belt of Truth

Paul begins with the belt: "Stand therefore, having your loins girt about with truth" (Eph. 6:14). In Roman armor, the belt held everything together. It gathered the loose tunic so the soldier could move freely. Without the belt, the rest of the armor would not fit properly.

Truth functions the same way in the believer's life. Satan's first and constant weapon is deception. He lied to Eve in Eden, he lied to Jesus in the wilderness, and he lies to every believer today. The belt of truth is the commitment to live in reality — the reality of who God is, the reality of what Christ has done, the reality of who we are in Him, and the reality of our own condition.

Putting on the belt of truth means several practical things. It means rejecting lies the moment they enter the mind. It means confessing sin honestly rather than hiding it. It means refusing to pretend that things are fine when they are not. It means speaking the truth in love to others and receiving it ourselves. A believer who is committed to truth is a believer whose armor is not slipping.

The belt also represents integrity. A life that is true on Sunday but false on Monday has no belt. The soldier whose tunic is loose will trip in battle. The Christian whose life is divided will fall when temptation comes. Tighten the belt every morning by deciding that today you will live without pretense before God and men.

## 4. The Breastplate of Righteousness

Next Paul names the breastplate: "having on the breastplate of righteousness" (Eph. 6:14). The breastplate protected the soldier's vital organs — heart, lungs, and torso. In spiritual warfare, the breastplate protects the believer's heart and conscience.

This righteousness is not self-righteousness. Paul has already made clear in Ephesians that we are saved by grace through faith, not by works (Eph. 2:8–9). The breastplate is the righteousness of Christ imputed to the believer, and it is also the practical righteousness that flows from a life submitted to God. Satan attacks the conscience. He brings accusations. He whispers that the believer is unworthy, unforgiven, and unusable. The breastplate answers every accusation with the finished work of Christ.

Romans 8:33–34 asks, "Who shall lay any thing to the charge of God's elect? It is God that justifieth. Who is he that condemneth?" The answer is no one, because Christ died and rose again. The believer who daily puts on the breastplate says, "I am not righteous because of my own perfection. I am righteous because of Jesus."

Practically, the breastplate also calls us to live righteously. If a believer is secretly indulging in sin, the breastplate has a crack in it. Confession, repentance, and walking in the light keep the breastplate solid. Do not let Satan use your own unconfessed sin as an entry point. Keep short accounts with God.

## 5. The Shoes of the Gospel of Peace

"Your feet shod with the preparation of the gospel of peace" (Eph. 6:15). Roman soldiers wore boots that protected their feet and gave them traction. A soldier with wounded or slipping feet cannot stand, cannot march, and cannot fight.

The gospel of peace is the foundation under the believer's feet. It is the confidence that we have peace with God through Jesus Christ (Rom. 5:1). It is the reminder that we are no longer enemies of God but His reconciled children. Satan wants the believer to feel unstable, uncertain, and anxious. The shoes of the gospel keep us standing on firm ground.

When a believer is anxious, he is easy to push over. When a believer is confident in God's love and Christ's finished work, he is hard to move. Put on the shoes by preaching the gospel to yourself every morning. Remind yourself that the war for your acceptance has already been won. Remind yourself that you are at peace with God.

The shoes also prepare us to move. A soldier's boots were for marching as well as standing. The believer who is shod with the gospel is ready to carry that gospel to others. Peace with God becomes peace for others. Be ready to explain the hope that is in you (1 Pet. 3:15).

## 6. The Shield of Faith

"Above all, taking the shield of faith, wherewith ye shall be able to quench all the fiery darts of the wicked" (Eph. 6:16). The shield Paul describes was a large, rectangular shield that covered most of the body. It was often soaked in water before battle so that flaming arrows would sizzle out on contact.

Faith is the shield because every attack of Satan is ultimately an attack on what we believe. The fiery darts are lies, accusations, temptations, fears, and doubts. They come fast and hot. The only thing that extinguishes them is faith — not faith in faith, but faith in the promises and person of God.

Faith lifts the shield when the believer says, "I believe what God has said more than I believe what I feel, what I see, or what the enemy suggests." When Satan says God does not love you, faith answers with Romans 8:38–39. When Satan says you will never overcome, faith answers with Philippians 4:13. When Satan says your future is hopeless, faith answers with Jeremiah 29:11 and the greater promises of the gospel.

The shield of faith must be taken up. It is not automatically in place. Every morning the believer must decide to trust God. Every lie must be answered with truth. Every dart must be quenched with confidence in God's Word.

## 7. The Helmet of Salvation

"Take the helmet of salvation" (Eph. 6:17). The helmet protects the head — the mind, the thoughts, the control center. Satan attacks the mind more than any other battlefield. If he can control your thinking, he can control your emotions, your decisions, and your destiny.

The helmet of salvation protects the believer's hope. Satan wants you to doubt your salvation. He wants you to think you are not secure, not forgiven, not kept by God. The helmet answers those thoughts with the assurance of salvation. "For God so loved the world, that he gave his only begotten Son, that whosoever believeth in him should not perish, but have everlasting life" (John 3:16). "And I give unto them eternal life; and they shall never perish, neither shall any man pluck them out of my hand" (John 10:28).

Wearing the helmet means guarding your mind. It means taking every thought captive to the obedience of Christ (2 Cor. 10:5). It means refusing to let your thoughts run wild in fear, lust, bitterness, or despair. It means feeding your mind with Scripture, worship, and truth so that there is no room for the lies.

## 8. The Sword of the Spirit: The Word of God

"And the sword of the Spirit, which is the word of God" (Eph. 6:17). The sword is the only offensive weapon in the armor. Every other piece is for protection. The sword is for striking back. It is the Word of God, quick and powerful and sharper than any two-edged sword (Heb. 4:12).

Jesus demonstrated the sword in the wilderness. Every temptation was answered with "It is written" (Matt. 4:4, 7, 10). He did not argue with Satan. He did not explain Himself. He quoted Scripture. That is the model. The believer who knows the Word can cut through deception, temptation, and accusation with authority.

The sword must be in your hand, not on a shelf. Many Christians own Bibles but do not wield them. They read devotionally but do not memorize strategically. In warfare, you need verses that come to mind instantly. You need promises, commands, and truths that you can speak out loud. The Holy Spirit brings the Word to remembrance (John 14:26), but He works with what you have put in your heart.

## 9. Prayer: The Power That Wields Every Piece

Paul does not end with the sword. He ends with prayer: "Praying always with all prayer and supplication in the Spirit, and watching thereunto with all perseverance and supplication for all saints" (Eph. 6:18). Prayer is what activates the armor. It is not a separate piece. It is the atmosphere in which every other piece functions.

Praying in the Spirit means praying under the Holy Spirit's guidance, in alignment with God's will, and with the help God provides. It means persistence. It means watchfulness. It means intercession not only for yourself but for all the saints. The soldier who is fully armed but does not communicate with headquarters is a soldier who will eventually fail.

Putting on the whole armor is therefore a prayerful act. Each morning, pray through the pieces. Ask God to gird you with truth, to clothe you with Christ's righteousness, to shod your feet with peace, to strengthen your shield of faith, to guard your mind with salvation, and to put the sword of the Spirit in your hand. Then walk into the day ready to stand.

## 10. Putting On the Armor as a Daily Discipline

Paul's command is present and continuous. "Put on the whole armour of God" implies a daily, repeated action. The armor is not put on once at conversion. It is put on every morning, and sometimes it must be adjusted throughout the day.

Make it a discipline. When you wake, before you touch your phone or begin your routine, speak the armor over yourself. Read Ephesians 6:10–18. Declare each piece. Ask the Holy Spirit to make every part real in your life that day. When you feel attacked, check your armor. Is your belt of truth tight? Is your shield of faith up? Is your helmet on?

The goal is not to become obsessed with the devil. The goal is to be ready so that you can live freely, serve faithfully, and worship fully. The armor keeps you standing so that you can do the work God has called you to do.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Read Ephesians 6:10–18 out loud every morning for one week. As you read, touch or gesture toward each corresponding part of your body.
2. Memorize one verse from the armor passage each week until you can quote the entire passage.
3. Identify which piece of armor you most often neglect. Ask God why and strengthen that area.
4. Write down three "fiery darts" Satan has thrown at you recently. Find a Scripture that answers each one.
5. Teach the whole armor to someone in your household or small group this week.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** This lesson teaches believers to daily put on every piece of spiritual armor from Ephesians 6 so they can stand firm against Satan's attacks through God's strength rather than their own.

**Key principle:** "Put on the whole armour of God, that ye may be able to stand against the wiles of the devil" (Ephesians 6:11). Victory comes from Christ's strength and equipment, not self-generated effort.

**Core teaching points:**
- The armor is God's provision for real spiritual conflict; the enemy is not flesh and blood.
- The belt of truth holds everything together by exposing lies and grounding the believer in reality.
- The breastplate of righteousness protects the conscience through Christ's imputed righteousness and a life of repentance.
- The shield of faith quenches every fiery dart by trusting God's promises above feelings, sight, or enemy suggestions.
- Prayer activates every piece of the armor and keeps the believer connected to God's direction and power.

**Real-world example:** A believer facing unexpected job loss feels the whisper that God has abandoned them. Instead of agreeing with the lie, they lift the shield of faith by declaring Romans 8:38-39, tighten the belt of truth by refusing self-pity, and stand until peace returns.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Treating the armor as a one-time prayer at conversion rather than a daily discipline that must be inspected, adjusted, and re-put-on every morning.

**Practice assignment:** Each morning this week, read Ephesians 6:10-18 aloud, touch or gesture toward each corresponding body part, and ask the Holy Spirit to make every piece real in your life that day.

**Worksheet idea:** Draw or list the seven pieces of armor. Beside each, write one lie Satan has thrown at you recently and the Scripture that answers it.

**Completion requirement:** The student prays through the armor daily for seven days and can identify at least one neglected piece with a specific plan to strengthen it.

## Questions on Lesson 37 — The Whole Armor of God

- Why does Paul call believers to "stand" rather than charge?
ANSWER: Because Christ has already won the territory; the armor keeps them upright under pressure.
- Which piece of the armor is the only offensive weapon?
ANSWER: The sword of the Spirit, which is the Word of God.
- What does it mean to gird your loins with truth?
ANSWER: To commit to live in spiritual reality, rejecting deception, pretense, and lies about God, yourself, and your condition.



---

### Lesson 38 — Resist the Devil: The Discipline of Steadfast Resistance

## 1. The Command to Resist

James writes with unusual directness: "Submit yourselves therefore to God. Resist the devil, and he will flee from you" (James 4:7). This is not a suggestion. It is a command with a promise. The command has two parts that belong together. First, submit to God. Second, resist the devil. You cannot successfully resist the devil while living in rebellion against God. Resistance flows from submission.

The promise is equally clear. The devil will flee. Not might flee. Not could flee. He will flee. This is not because the believer is impressive. It is because the believer is standing in the authority of the One whom Satan cannot defeat. When a submitted believer resists, the devil has no legal ground, no spiritual power, and no lasting strategy.

Peter adds the posture of steadfastness. "Be sober, be vigilant; because your adversary the devil, as a roaring lion, walketh about, seeking whom he may devour: whom resist stedfast in the faith" (1 Pet. 5:8–9). Steadfastness means firmness, endurance, and refusal to move. The believer does not resist once and then relax. He resists steadfastly in the faith.

## 2. What Resistance Looks Like in Daily Life

Resistance is not theatrical. It does not require shouting, dramatic gestures, or emotional intensity. Resistance is the settled refusal to agree with, cooperate with, or bow to Satan's lies, temptations, accusations, and pressures. It is the consistent choice to obey God instead.

When a tempting thought enters your mind, resistance says no and turns the attention to Christ. When an accusation whispers that you are worthless, resistance answers with the blood of Jesus. When fear tries to control a decision, resistance stands on God's promises. When bitterness toward someone rises up, resistance chooses forgiveness. When laziness or compromise invites you to skip prayer, resistance says, "I will seek God first."

Resistance is active. It is not passively waiting for the feeling to pass. It is speaking, deciding, moving, and praying. Jesus said to Peter, "Simon, Simon, behold, Satan hath desired to have you, that he may sift you as wheat: but I have prayed for thee, that thy faith fail not" (Luke 22:31–32). Faith that does not fail is faith that resists.

## 3. Matthew 4:11: The Outcome of Resistance

The wilderness temptation shows resistance at its highest level. Satan offered Jesus bread, prestige, and power. Jesus answered each offer with Scripture. He did not compromise. He did not negotiate. He did not explain why the offers were tempting. He simply refused. "Then the devil leaveth him, and, behold, angels came and ministered unto him" (Matt. 4:11).

Notice the sequence. Resistance came first. The departure of the devil came second. The ministry of angels came third. The believer who resists will find that the enemy leaves and that heaven's provision arrives. You may not see angels, but you will see grace, peace, and strength return.

This pattern is repeated in the lives of God's people. Joseph resisted Potiphar's wife and was thrown into prison — but God eventually exalted him. Daniel resisted the pressure to stop praying and was thrown into the lions' den — but God shut the lions' mouths. Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego refused to bow — but God walked with them in the fire. Resistance is costly in the short term, but it is always vindicated by God.

## 4. Steadfastness in the Faith

Peter's command to resist "stedfast in the faith" deserves special attention. The word "stedfast" means solid, immovable, and firmly fixed. It is the opposite of wavering. James warns that the one who doubts is like a wave of the sea, driven and tossed by the wind (James 1:6). Satan loves a wavering believer because he can push him in any direction.

Steadfastness comes from knowing what you believe and why you believe it. It comes from being rooted in Scripture, connected to the body of Christ, and committed to obedience. The believer who is steadfast does not change with every new circumstance. He does not abandon his confession because life became hard. He stands.

The phrase "in the faith" also matters. We resist not in our own strength or opinion but in the faith — the body of truth entrusted to the church. Jude urged believers to "earnestly contend for the faith which was once delivered unto the saints" (Jude 3). When we resist, we are standing on the apostolic gospel, not on private ideas.

## 5. The Discipline of Refusal

One of the most practical expressions of resistance is the discipline of refusal. Learn to say no quickly. When temptation presents itself, do not linger. Do not imagine. Do not reason with it. Refuse it immediately. Joseph did not negotiate with Potiphar's wife. He ran. Paul told Timothy to "flee youthful lusts" (2 Tim. 2:22). Fleeing is a form of resisting.

The same discipline applies to thoughts. When a lie enters, refuse it. When a sinful image appears, reject it. When a memory of past failure is thrown at you, refuse to let it define you. You do not have to accept every thought that knocks on the door of your mind. You can refuse entry.

This discipline must be practiced. Resistance is a muscle. The more you use it, the stronger it becomes. The believer who has a habit of immediate refusal will find that temptations lose their power. The believer who delays, debates, and entertains temptation will eventually fall.

## 6. Resisting Together

Peter wrote his warning to a community of believers. "Whom resist stedfast in the faith, knowing that the same afflictions are accomplished in your brethren that are in the world" (1 Pet. 5:9). We do not resist alone. The same battle is being fought by Christians everywhere. This shared struggle is meant to produce compassion, prayer, and mutual strengthening, not isolation.

There is power in agreeing resistance. Jesus said, "If two of you shall agree on earth as touching any thing that they shall ask, it shall be done for them of my Father which is in heaven. For where two or three are gathered together in my name, there am I in the midst of them" (Matt. 18:19–20). When believers agree in resistance, the presence and authority of Christ are manifest.

Find people who will stand with you. Confess your struggles to a trusted believer. Ask for prayer. Do not pretend that resistance is easy or that you never need help. The church is a fellowship of soldiers, and soldiers fight better together than alone.

## 7. Now Apply It

1. Read James 4:7 and 1 Peter 5:8–9 every morning this week. Memorize both passages.
2. Identify one area where you have been passive instead of resistant. Decide today that you will actively refuse that temptation or lie.
3. When the next accusation comes, answer it aloud with a specific Scripture about your identity in Christ.
4. Ask one mature believer to be your "resistance partner" — someone you can call or text when under attack.
5. Review Matthew 4:1–11 and list the three ways Jesus resisted. Apply one of them to a current battle.

---

## At a Glance

**Summary:** This lesson grounds spiritual resistance in submission to God, teaching believers to refuse Satan's lies and temptations consistently so that the devil flees and God's provision arrives.

**Key principle:** "Submit yourselves therefore to God. Resist the devil, and he will flee from you" (James 4:7). Submission creates the foundation; resistance releases the promise.

**Core teaching points:**
- Resistance flows from submission to God; you cannot resist the devil while living in rebellion against God.
- "Steadfast in the faith" means firm, immovable resistance rooted in the apostolic gospel, not private opinion.
- Resistance is active refusal: saying no to temptation, answering accusations with Scripture, and choosing obedience immediately.
- Resistance is costly in the short term but vindicated by God, as seen in Joseph, Daniel, and the three Hebrews.
- Believers resist more effectively together through confession, agreement, and intercession.

**Real-world example:** A woman tempted to revisit a harmful relationship recognizes the lie, immediately refuses it, texts her resistance partner for prayer, and redirects her attention to Scripture and worship until the pressure subsides.

## Practice & Assessment

**Common student mistake:** Waiting for the feeling of resistance to appear before acting, which allows temptation to linger, reason, and eventually win.

**Practice assignment:** Identify one area where you have been passive. Decide today that you will actively refuse that temptation or lie, and ask one mature believer to be your "resistance partner" this week.
